v(t):=\mathcal{L}(t,s;z)\varphi\quad\text{is the unique solution to}\quad\left\{\begin{aligned} &i{\partial}_{t}v+Hv=B[z]v,\quad\\
&v(s)=\varphi.\end{aligned}\right.

Then
\mathcal{L}(t,s;z)
is a bounded operator, which is
\mathbb{R}
-linear but not
\mathbb{C}
-linear (except for the case
\mathcal{L}(t,s;0)=e^{i(t-s)H}
).

We then have the following:

Lemma 4.4
.

For
z\in\mathrm{SBC}
,
\mathcal{L}(t,s;z)
obeys the following: for any
t,s,r\in\mathbb{R}
,

•

\mathcal{L}(t,t;z)=\mathrm{Id}
;

•

\mathcal{L}(t,s;z)\mathcal{L}(s,r;z)=\mathcal{L}(t,r;z)
;

•

\mathcal{L}(t,s;z)=\mathcal{L}(t-r,s-r;z(\cdot+r))
.

For the inhomogeneous problem, we have the following Duhamel formula.

Proposition 4.5

(Duhamel formula)
.

Let
z\in\mathrm{SBC}
,
I\subset\mathbb{R}
, and
N:I\to P_{c}H^{1}
. The
w(t)
to the inhomogeneous linearized equation

\left\{\begin{aligned} &i{\partial}_{t}w+Hw=B[z]w+N,\quad\\
&w(s)=\varphi\end{aligned}\right.

is given by

w(t)=\mathcal{L}(t,s;z)\varphi+\mathcal{D}(t,s;z)N,

(4.1)

where

\mathcal{D}(t,s;z)N:=-\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{L}(t,r;z)(iN(r))dr

(4.2)

In general we have
\mathcal{L}(t,r;z)(iN(r))\neq i\mathcal{L}(t,r;z)N(r)
, as
\mathcal{L}(t,s;z)
is not
\mathbb{C}
-linear.

For the usual Strichartz spaces, we follow
[
19
]
and define

\mathrm{Str}^{s}:=L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}\cap L^{2}_{t}B^{s}_{6,2},\quad\mathrm{Str}^{*s}:=L^{1}_{t}H^{s}_{x}+L^{2}_{t}B^{s}_{6/5,2}

(4.3)

and observe the embeddings
\mathrm{Str}^{1/2}\hookrightarrow L^{4}_{t}B^{1/2}_{3,2}\hookrightarrow L^{4}_{t}L^{6}_{x}
. We record here the following uniform Strichartz estimate for the linearized flow from
[
19
, Lemma 4.3]
. Here the uniformity is with respect to the choice of
z\in\mathrm{SBC}
.

Proposition 4.6

(Uniform Strichartz estimate)
.

Let
z\in\mathrm{SBC}
and
I\subset\mathbb{R}
. Then for any
\varphi\in P_{c}H^{1}
and
\theta\in[0,1]
, we have

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z)\varphi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{H^{\theta}}.

Furthermore,

\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,0;z)\varphi\right\rVert_{H^{\theta}}\sim\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{H^{\theta}}\sim\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,0;z)\varphi\right\rVert_{H^{\theta}}.

Finally, for any
s_{0}\in I
,

\left\lVert\mathcal{D}(\cdot,s_{0};z)(P_{c}F)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}(I)}\lesssim\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta*}(I)}.

In the above estimates, the implicit constants are independent of the choice of
z(t)
.

The next lemma also a consequence of
[
19
, Lemma 4.3]
. For the sake of completeness, we provide a proof here.

Lemma 4.7
.

For any
\varphi\in P_{c}H^{1}
,

\sup_{z\in\mathrm{SBC}}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,0;z)\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([0,\infty))}\lesssim\inf_{z\in\mathrm{SBC}}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,0;z)\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([0,\infty))}

A similar estimate holds on the interval
(-\infty,0]
.

Proof.

Let
z\in\mathrm{SBC}
and
\varphi\in P_{c}H^{1}
. Writing
u(t)=\mathcal{L}(t,0;z)\varphi
, we use the Duhamel with respect to
e^{itH}
to write

u(t)=e^{itH}\varphi-i\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-s)H}B[z(s)]u(s)\,ds.

Thus, using the Strichartz estimate for
e^{itH}P_{c}
, we have

\left\lVert u-e^{itH}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([0,\infty))}\leqslant C\left\lVert z\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([0,\infty))}\ll\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([0,\infty))},

where the implicit small constant depends only on upper bound on
\left\lVert z\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R})}
. The result follows. ∎

We also need the following vanishing lemma.

Proposition 4.8
.

For any
\varphi\in P_{c}H^{1}
,

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\sup_{z\in\mathrm{SBC}}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,0;z)\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}((-\infty,-\tau]\cup[\tau,\infty))}=0.

Proof.

It suffices to consider the forward time direction. If the proposition fails, then there exists a sequence
\tau_{n}\to\infty
,
z_{n}\in\mathrm{SBC}
, and
c_{0}>0
such that

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([\tau_{n},\infty))}\geqslant c_{0}.

(4.4)

Passing to a subsequence,
z_{n}
converges locally uniformly to some
z_{\infty}\in\mathrm{SBC}
. We then choose
\tau^{*}>0
so that

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{\infty})\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([\tau^{*},\infty))}\leqslant\tfrac{c_{0}}{3C_{0}},

where
C_{0}
is the implicit constant in the preceding lemma.

Now set
u_{n}(t)=\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})\varphi
. By the Duhamel formula with respect to

\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{\infty})
, one has

u_{n}(t)=\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{\infty})\varphi-\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{L}(t,s;z_{\infty})i((B[z_{n}(s)]-B[z_{\infty}(s)])u_{n}(s))ds

By the uniform Strichartz estimate, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert u_{n}(t)-\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{\infty})\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}H^{1}([0,\tau^{*}])}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert(B[z_{n}]-B[z_{\infty}])u_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6/5}([0,\tau^{*}])}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert z_{n}-z_{\infty}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau^{*}])}\left\lVert u_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert z_{n}-z_{\infty}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau^{*}])}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

Thus, by the preceding lemma and the Strichartz estimate, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle(t,0;z_{n})\varphi\|_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([\tau^{*},\infty))}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n}(\cdot+\tau^{*}))\mathcal{L}(\tau^{*},0;z_{n})\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([0,\infty))}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{0}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{\infty}(\cdot+\tau^{*}))\mathcal{L}(\tau^{*},0;z_{n})\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([0,\infty))}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,\tau^{*};z_{\infty})\mathcal{L}(\tau^{*},0;z_{n})\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([\tau^{*},\infty))}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{0}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{\infty})\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([\tau^{*},\infty))}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,\tau^{*};z_{\infty})(u_{n}(\tau^{*})-\mathcal{L}(\tau^{*},0;{z_{\infty}})\varphi)\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([\tau^{*},\infty))}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tfrac{c_{0}}{3}+o_{n}(1)\leqslant\tfrac{2c_{0}}{3}

for large
n
, which contradicts (
4.4
) since
\tau_{n}>\tau^{*}
for large
n
. ∎

Finally, we need a Strichartz estimate with non-admissible pairs from
[
19
]
.

Lemma 4.9

(Non-admissible Strichartz estimate)
.

Let
(q_{0},r_{0})
,
(q_{1},r_{1})\in(1,\infty)\times(2,6]
and
\sigma_{j}=\tfrac{2}{q_{j}}+3(\tfrac{1}{r_{j}}-\tfrac{1}{2})
satisfy

\sigma_{0}+\sigma_{1}=0,\quad\max_{j=0,1}(\sigma_{j}-\tfrac{1}{q_{j}})<0,\quad|\sigma_{j}|\leqslant\tfrac{2}{3}.

Then, for any
z\in\mathrm{SBC}
and
s_{0}\in I\subset\mathbb{R}
,

\left\lVert\mathcal{D}(\cdot,s_{0};z)(P_{c}F)\right\rVert_{L^{q_{0}}L^{r_{0}}(I)}\lesssim\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}L^{r_{1}^{\prime}}(I)}.

Proof.

The proof is based on the argument in
[
31
]
. We first see that the estimate holds for the case
V\equiv 0
by
[
7
,
30
]
. Then, by assumption (A4), we see that the wave operator
W=\lim_{t\to\infty}e^{itH}e^{it\Delta}
and its adjoint
W^{\ast}
are bounded in
L^{p}
,
2\leqslant p\leqslant 6
. Combining these facts, we obtain the estimate.
∎

At a few points we will also make use of the following dispersive estimates for
\mathcal{L}
.

Lemma 4.10
.

The following estimates hold uniformly for
z\in\mathrm{SBC}
: For any
2\leq p_{1}<6<p_{2}{\leq p}
, where
p
is given by Assumption
(A4)
and
t\neq s
,

\|\mathcal{L}(t,s;z)P_{c}\varphi\|_{L^{p_{1}}+L^{p_{2}}}\lesssim f(t-s)\|\varphi\|_{L^{p_{1}^{\prime}}\cap L^{p_{2}^{\prime}}},\quad f(t):=\min_{j=1,2}\bigl\{|t|^{-(\frac{3}{2}-\frac{3}{p_{j}})}\bigr\}.

Proof.

The assumptions on
V
imply that the standard dispersive estimates hold for
e^{-itH}
. We will use the Duhamel formula and the smallness of
z\in\mathrm{SBC}
to transfer the estimates to
\mathcal{L}(t,0;z)
. Writing
u(t)=\mathcal{L}(t,0;z)P_{c}\varphi,
we have

u(t)=e^{itH}P_{c}\varphi-i\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-s)H}B[z(s)]u(s)\,ds.

We note that by translating
z
, we may assume
s=0
. Then, noting that

\|B[z(s)]u\|_{L^{p_{1}^{\prime}}\cap L^{p_{2}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|z\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|u\|_{L^{p_{1}}+L^{p_{2}}},

we fix
T>0
and
t\in[0,T]
and use the dispersive estimate for
e^{itH}
to obtain

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{f(t)}

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{L^{p_{1}}+L^{p_{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{p_{1}^{\prime}}\cap L^{p_{2}^{\prime}}}+\tfrac{1}{f(t)}\int_{0}^{t}f(t-s)f(s)\,ds\cdot\|z\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}}^{2}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\tfrac{1}{f(t)}\|u(t)\|_{L^{p_{1}}+L^{p_{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{p_{1}^{\prime}}\cap L^{p_{2}^{\prime}}}+c\biggl[\tfrac{1}{f(t)}\int_{0}^{t}f(t-s)f(s)\,ds\biggr]\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\tfrac{1}{f(t)}\|u(t)\|_{L^{p_{1}}+L^{p_{2}}}

for some
0<c\ll 1
. Thus, the result follows as before, provided we have that

\sup_{t>0}\tfrac{1}{f(t)}\int_{0}^{t}f(t-s)f(s)\,ds\lesssim 1.

Indeed, the assumption that
p_{1}<6<p_{2}
guarantees that
f\in L_{t}^{1}
, and so the estimate follows by observing that

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{f(t)}\int_{0}^{t}f(t-s)f(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\tfrac{1}{f(t)}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t/2}f(t)f(s)\,ds+\int_{t/2}^{t}f(t-s)f(t)\,ds\biggr]\lesssim\|f\|_{L_{t}^{1}}

uniformly in
t>0
.∎

We next consider the equation

i{\partial}_{t}\xi+H\xi=B[z]\xi+\tilde{N}(z,\xi)+\mathcal{E}

(4.5)

where
\tilde{N}
is as in (
3.7
) and
\mathcal{E}=P_{c}\mathcal{E}
is a perturbative term. We regard
z\in\mathrm{SBC}
as given and view this as an equation for
\xi
. In what follows, we will utilize some semi-norms introduced in
[
19
]
. Given
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
and
z\in C(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C})
, we first define
\mathcal{L}_{>}(s_{0},z)
:
C(\mathbb{R};H^{1})\to C(\mathbb{R};H^{1})
by

[\mathcal{L}_{>}(s,z)u](t)=\begin{cases}u(t)&t<s_{0},\\
\mathcal{L}(t,s_{0};z)(u|_{t=s_{0}})&t\geqslant s_{0}.\end{cases}

(4.6)

Thus
\mathcal{L}_{>}(s_{0},z)
maps a function to a solution to
i\partial_{t}v+Hv=B[z]v
with
z(t)
in the interval
(s_{0},\infty)
in such a way that the resulting function is continuous at
t=s_{0}
. We may omit
z
if it is clear from the context. We then define

\displaystyle\|u\|_{[z;T_{0},T_{1};T_{2}]}:=\sup_{T_{0}<S<T<T_{1}}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}_{>}(T,z)u-\mathcal{L}_{>}(S,z)u\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}((T_{0},T_{2})\times\mathbb{R}^{3})},

(4.7)

\displaystyle\|u\|_{[z;T_{0},T_{1};T_{2}]^{\prime}}:=\sup_{T_{0}<T<T_{1}}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}_{>}(T,z)u-\mathcal{L}_{>}(T_{0},z)u\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}((T_{0},T_{2})\times\mathbb{R}^{3})},

which are equivalent semi-norms. In particular, these semi-norms are zero precisely when
u
is a solution to
i\partial_{t}u+Hu=B[z]u
on
(T_{0},T_{1})
, so that these semi-norms provide a measure of nonlinear contributions.

We turn to the stability results. The first result concerns small solutions to the nonlinear problem.

Proposition 4.11

(Perturbation around zero)
.

Let
-\infty<T_{0}<T_{1}\leqslant\infty
,
z\in\mathrm{SBC}
, and
\varphi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Let
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{0}*}(T_{0},T_{1})
for some
\theta_{0}\in[\tfrac{1}{2},1]
, and set

\mathcal{N}_{\theta}:=\left\lVert z\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}([T_{0},T_{1}))}+\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{H^{\theta}}+\left\lVert\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}([T_{0},T_{1}))}

for
\theta\in[0,1]
, where
\mathcal{D}
is as in (
4.2
). Suppose that
\mathcal{N}_{0}\ll 1
.

\mathrm{(i)}
If

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}{(T_{0},T_{1})}}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\frac{1}{2}}\ll 1,

(4.8)

then (
4.5
) with
\xi(T_{0})=\varphi
has a unique solution
\xi\in C^{1}([T_{0},T_{1});H^{\theta_{0}})
satisfying

\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(T_{0},T_{1})}\lesssim\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(T_{0},T_{1})}

and

\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}(T_{0},T_{1})}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{\theta}\quad\text{for all}\quad\theta\in[0,\theta_{0}].

\mathrm{(ii)}
If the solution
\xi\in C^{1}([T_{0},T_{1});H^{\theta_{0}})
to (
4.5
) with
\xi(T_{0})=\varphi
satisfies

\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}{(T_{0},T_{1})}}\mathcal{N}_{\frac{1}{2}}\ll 1

then the following estimates hold: for any
T\in(T_{0},T_{1})
and
\theta\in[0,\theta_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{>}(T,z)\xi-\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi-

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\|_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}(T_{0},T_{1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{\theta}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(T_{0},T)}

\displaystyle\quad\times(\mathcal{N}_{0}+\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(T_{0},T)}),

\displaystyle\|\xi-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\|_{{[z;T_{0},T,T_{1}]}}

\displaystyle\ll\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(T_{0},T)}

\displaystyle\sim\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(T_{0},T)}.

Proof.

(i) As the equation is
H^{\frac{1}{2}}
-critical, it is straightforward to obtain a local solution if
\theta_{0}\geqslant\tfrac{1}{2}
. We need to obtain a solution on the whole interval
[T_{0},T_{1}]
and prove the desired bound.

First, using the Strichartz estimate, we have

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{\theta}\quad\text{for any}\quad\theta\in[0,1],

and (recalling the definition of
\tilde{N}
from (
3.7
))

\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leqslant C\mathcal{N}_{\frac{1}{2}}+C\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}(\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\left\lVert z\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}).

Now, by the non-admissible Strichartz estimates of Lemma
4.9
, we have

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\xi\|_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+{\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}}+C\left\lVert\tilde{N}(z,\xi)\right\rVert_{L^{8/3}L^{4/3}+L^{4}L^{6/5}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+{\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}}+C\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}^{2}(\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}+\left\lVert\Phi[z]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}L^{3}})

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+{\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}}+C\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}^{2}(\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\left\lVert z\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}).

Thus, by continuity, there exists
\delta>0
such that on
(T_{0},T_{0}+\delta)
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{}

\displaystyle\leqslant 100C\mathcal{N}_{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}{}

\displaystyle\leqslant 100\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}},

where
C\geqslant 1
is the same constant as in the above estimates.

Recalling the estimates above and (
4.8
), we have that on the same interval,

\displaystyle\|\xi\|_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\mathcal{N}_{\frac{1}{2}}+C\bigl\{\|\xi\|_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\|z\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}}\bigr\}\|\xi\|_{L^{8}L^{4}}\|\xi\|_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\mathcal{N}_{\frac{1}{2}}+100^{2}C^{2}\cdot(100C+1)\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}\mathcal{N}_{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2C\mathcal{N}_{\frac{1}{2}}.

Similarly, one has

\displaystyle\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}\leqslant{}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\varepsilon\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}

\displaystyle{}+100^{2}C[100C+1]\mathcal{N}_{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}\cdot\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}},

which yields

\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}\leqslant 2\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}.

By a continuity argument, we deduce the desired bounds on
(T_{0},T_{1})
.

Finally, for any
\theta\in[0,1]
, we may obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}}\leqslant C\mathcal{N}_{\theta}+4^{2}C^{2}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}\mathcal{N}_{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}},

which implies the desired bound
\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}}\leqslant 2C\mathcal{N}_{\theta}.

(ii) To begin, an application of Strichartz yields

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{R})}\leqslant C\mathcal{N}_{\frac{1}{2}}.

Further, there exists
\delta>0
such that

\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1/2}(T_{0},T_{0}+\delta)}\leqslant 100C\mathcal{N}_{1/2}

with the same constant as above.
Hence. on the same interval,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle{}\leqslant C\mathcal{N}_{1/2}+C\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1/2}}\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}(\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1/2}}+\left\lVert z\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}})

\displaystyle{}\leqslant C\mathcal{N}_{1/2}+100C^{2}\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}\mathcal{N}_{1/2}\times\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1/2}}.

The second term on the right can be absorbed by the left by assumption. Thus

\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1/2}}\leqslant 2C\mathcal{N}_{\frac{1}{2}}.

By a continuation argument, the last estimate is true on the interval
(T_{0},T_{1})
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\xi-\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}^{2}(\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}+\left\lVert z\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}})

\displaystyle{}\lesssim\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}(\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}\mathcal{N}_{1/2})\ll\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}

by assumption. Consequently,

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(T_{0},T_{1})}\sim\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(T_{0},T_{1})}.

Note that the same estimate is valid in any subinterval of the form
(T_{0},T)
.

Now fix
T\in(T_{0},T_{1})
.
By Strichartz,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\xi-\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}(T_{0},T)}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}(T_{0},T)}\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(T_{0},T)}(\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(T_{0},T)}+\left\lVert z\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}})

\displaystyle{}\lesssim\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}(T_{0},T)}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(T_{0},T_{1})}

\displaystyle\quad\times(\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(T_{0},T_{1})}+\mathcal{N}_{0}).

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T;z)(\xi(T)-

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(T,T_{0})\varphi-\mathcal{D}(T,T_{0})\mathcal{E})\|_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}(T,T_{1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert\xi(T)-\mathcal{L}(T,T_{0})\varphi-\mathcal{D}(T,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{H^{\theta}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\xi-\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi-\mathcal{D}(T,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}(T_{0},T)}.

Thus we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{L}_{>}(z,T)\xi-\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})({\bf 1}_{(-\infty,T)}\mathcal{E})\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta}(T_{0},,T_{1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{\theta}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(T_{0},T_{1})}

\displaystyle\quad\times(\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(T_{0},T_{1})}+\mathcal{N}_{0}).

Now let
T^{\prime}\in(T_{0},T)
. We then have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{L}_{>}(z,T^{\prime})(\xi-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E})-\mathcal{L}_{>}(z,T_{0})(\xi-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(T_{0},T_{1})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\left\lVert\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})({\bf 1}_{[T_{0},T^{\prime})}\tilde{N})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(T_{0},T_{1})}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}[T_{0},T^{\prime})}(\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}[T_{0},T_{1})}\mathcal{N}_{1/2})

\displaystyle\quad\ll\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(T_{0},T)}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(T_{0},T)}.

Taking the supremum with respect to
T^{\prime}\in(T_{0},T)
, we obtain

\left\lVert\xi-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{[z;T_{0},T,T_{1}]}\ll\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0})\varphi+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0})\mathcal{E}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(T_{0},T)}.

∎

We turn to stability for the nonlinear equation.

Lemma 4.12

(Stability, I)
.

Let
-\infty<T_{0}<T_{1}<\infty
and let
z_{0},z_{1}\in\mathrm{SBC}
. Suppose
\xi_{0},\xi_{1}\in C_{t}P_{c}H^{1}([T_{0},T_{1}])
solve

(i{\partial}_{t}+H)\xi_{j}=B[z_{j}]\xi_{j}+\tilde{N}(z_{j},\xi_{j})+P_{c}\mathcal{E}_{j}

on
[T_{0},T_{1}]
, respectively, where
\mathcal{E}_{j}\in L^{\frac{8}{3}}L^{\frac{4}{3}}([T_{0},T_{1}])
is given. Suppose further that

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0},z_{0})\xi_{0}(T_{0})+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0};z_{0})\mathcal{E}_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([T_{0},T_{1}])}\leqslant\tilde{\delta}

for some small
\tilde{\delta}>0
. If

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0},z_{0})(\xi_{0}(T_{0})-\xi_{1}(T_{0}))+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0};z_{0})(\mathcal{E}_{0}

\displaystyle-\mathcal{E}_{1})\|_{L^{8}L^{4}([T_{0},T_{1}])}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert z_{0}-z_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8}(T_{0},T_{1})}\leqslant{\delta},

then the following hold:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\xi_{0}-\xi_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([T_{0},T_{1}])}\lesssim\delta,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\xi_{0}-\xi_{1}-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0};z_{0})(\mathcal{E}_{0}-\mathcal{E}_{1})\right\rVert_{[z,T_{0},T_{1};T_{2}]}\lesssim\tilde{\delta}\delta.

Proof.

Set

\mathrm{Dist}(t)=\sum_{j=0}^{1}(-1)^{j}(\xi_{j}(t)-\mathcal{L}(t,T_{0},z_{0})\xi_{j}(T_{0})-\mathcal{D}(t,T_{0};z_{0})\mathcal{E}_{j}).

By the Duhamel formula for the linearized equation, we obtain

\xi_{0}=\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0};z_{0})\xi_{0}(T_{0})+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0},z_{0})\tilde{N}(z_{0},\xi_{0})+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0},z_{0})\mathcal{E}_{0}

and

\xi_{1}=\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0};z_{0})\xi_{1}(T_{0})+[\mathcal{D}(T_{0},z_{0})(\tilde{N}(z_{1},\xi_{1})+(B[z_{1}]-B[z_{0}])\xi_{1})]+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0},z_{0})\mathcal{E}_{1}.

By the non-admissible Strichartz estimate (Lemma
4.9
),

\left\lVert\mathrm{Dist}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}\lesssim\left\lVert(\tilde{N}(z_{0},\xi_{0})-\tilde{N}(z_{1},\xi_{1}))+(B[z_{0}]-B[z_{1}])\xi_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8/3}L^{4/3}+L^{4}L^{6/5}}.

Using the estimate

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\tilde{N}(z_{0},\xi_{0})-\tilde{N}(z_{1},\xi_{1}))+(B[z_{0}]-B[z_{1}])\xi_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8/3}L^{4/3}+L^{4}L^{6/5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\left\lVert\xi_{0}-\xi_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}+\left\lVert z_{0}-z_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8}})(\left\lVert\xi_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}+\left\lVert\xi_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}})

\displaystyle\quad\times(1+\left\lVert\xi_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}+\left\lVert\xi_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}),

we obtain

\left\lVert\mathrm{Dist}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(T_{0},T_{1})}\lesssim\tilde{\delta}\left\lVert\xi_{0}-\xi_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(T_{0},T_{1})}+\delta\tilde{\delta}.

If
\tilde{\delta}
is sufficiently small, then we have

\displaystyle\|\xi_{0}

\displaystyle-\xi_{1}\|_{L^{8}L^{4}(T_{0},T_{1})}

(4.9)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,T_{0},z_{0})(\xi_{0}-\xi_{1})(T_{0})+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0};z_{0})(\mathcal{E}_{0}-\mathcal{E}_{1})\right\rVert_{L^{4}L^{24/7}(T_{0},T_{1})}+\delta\tilde{\delta}\lesssim\delta.

Next, note that

\mathcal{L}(t,T_{1};z_{0})\xi_{0}(T_{1})=\mathcal{L}(t,T_{0};z_{0})\xi_{0}(T_{0})+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0};z_{0})({\bf 1}_{(-\infty,T_{1}]}\mathcal{E}_{0})\\
+\mathcal{L}(t,T_{1};z_{0})\mathcal{D}(T_{1},T_{0},z_{0})\tilde{N}(z_{0},\xi_{0})

and

\mathcal{L}(t,T_{1};z_{0})\xi_{1}(T_{1})=\mathcal{L}(t,T_{0};z_{0})\xi_{1}(T_{0})+\mathcal{D}(t,T_{0};z_{0})({\bf 1}_{(-\infty,T_{1}]}\mathcal{E}_{1})\\
+\mathcal{L}(t,T_{1};z_{0})\mathcal{D}(T_{1},T_{0},z_{0})(\tilde{N}(z_{1},\xi_{1})+(B[z_{1}]-B[z_{0}])\xi_{1}).

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(t,T_{1};z_{0})(\xi_{0}(T_{1})-\xi_{1}(T_{1}))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(t,T_{0};z_{0})(\xi_{0}(T_{0})-\xi_{1}(T_{0}))+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0};z_{0})({\bf 1}_{(-\infty,T_{1}]}(\mathcal{E}_{0}-\mathcal{E}_{1}))

\displaystyle{}\quad+\mathcal{D}(t,T_{0},z_{0})({\bf 1}_{[T_{0},T_{1}]}(\tilde{N}(z_{1},\xi_{1})+(B[z_{1}]-B[z_{0}])\xi_{1})).

We now define

\displaystyle\mathrm{Diff}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{L}_{>}(t,T_{1};z_{0})(\xi_{0}-\xi_{1})-\mathcal{L}(t,T_{0};z_{0})(\xi_{0}-\xi_{1})(T_{0})-

\displaystyle\quad-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0};z_{0})({\bf 1}_{(-\infty,T_{1}]}(\mathcal{E}_{0}-\mathcal{E}_{1}))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{>}(t,T_{1};z_{0})(\xi_{0}-\xi_{1}-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0};z_{0})(\mathcal{E}_{0}-\mathcal{E}_{1})

\displaystyle\quad-\mathcal{L}_{>}(t,T_{0};z_{0})(\xi_{0}-\xi_{1}-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0};z_{0})(\mathcal{E}_{0}-\mathcal{E}_{1}))

for
t\geqslant T_{0}
.
We use this form for
t\geqslant T_{1}
and the above formulas for
\xi_{0}
and
\xi_{1}
for
t\in[T_{0},T_{1}]
to get

\mathrm{Diff}=\mathcal{D}(T_{0},z_{0})({\bf 1}_{[T_{0},T_{1}]}(\tilde{N}(z_{1},\xi_{1})+(B[z_{1}]-B[z_{0}])\xi_{1})).

for
t\geqslant T_{0}
.
Thus, by the same nonlinear estimate as for
\left\lVert\mathrm{Dist}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(T_{0},T_{1})}
, we may obtain

\left\lVert\mathrm{Diff}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([T_{0},T_{2}])}\lesssim\tilde{\delta}\left\lVert\xi_{0}-\xi_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(T_{0},T_{1})}+\delta\tilde{\delta}\lesssim\delta\tilde{\delta}.

Taking the supremum with respect to
T_{1}
, we obtain the result.
∎

We upgrade Lemma
4.12
as follows:

Proposition 4.13

(Stability, II)
.

Let
-\infty<T_{0}<T_{1}<T_{2}\leqslant\infty
,
z_{0},z_{1}\in\mathrm{SBC}
,
and
\xi_{0},\xi_{1}\in C([T_{0},T_{1}];H^{3/4})
solve

i{\partial}_{t}\xi_{j}+H\xi_{j}=B[z_{j}]\xi_{j}+\tilde{N}(z_{j},\xi_{j})+\mathcal{E}_{j}

on
[T_{0},T_{1}]
, respectively, where
\mathcal{E}_{j}\in L^{8/3}L^{4/3}([T_{0},T_{1}])
are given. Let

\mathcal{M}:=\left\lVert\xi_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(T_{0},T_{1})}.

There exists
\delta_{*}(\mathcal{M})>0
and
C_{*}(\mathcal{M})>0

such that if

\displaystyle\delta:=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,T_{0},z_{0})(\xi_{0}-\xi_{1})(T_{0})+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0},z_{0})(\mathcal{E}_{0}-\mathcal{E}_{1})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([T_{0},T_{1}])}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert z_{0}-z_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8}(T_{0},T_{1})}\leqslant\delta_{*},

then we have

\left\lVert\xi_{0}-\xi_{1}-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0},z_{0})(\mathcal{E}_{0}-\mathcal{E}_{1})\right\rVert_{[z_{0};T_{0},T_{1};T_{2}]}\leqslant C_{*}\delta.

Proof.

For any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
(T_{0},T_{1})
may be decomposed into subintervals
I_{0},\dots,I_{N}
of the form
I_{j}=(S_{j},S_{j+1})

such that

\sup_{j\in[1,N]}\left\lVert\xi_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{4}L^{6}(I_{j})}\leqslant 2N^{-\frac{1}{8}}\mathcal{M}=:\tilde{\delta}.

If
N
is chosen sufficiently large, we see that

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,S_{j};z_{0})\xi_{0}(S_{j})+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,S_{j};z_{0})\mathcal{E}_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(I_{j})}\sim\left\lVert\xi_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(I_{j})}\leqslant\tilde{\delta}.

Now let
\delta_{0}\in(0,\tilde{\delta}]
to be chosen later. Suppose that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,S_{0},z_{0})(\xi_{0}-\xi_{1})(T_{0})+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,S_{0},z_{0})(\mathcal{E}_{0}-\mathcal{E}_{1})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([S_{0},T_{1}])}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert z_{0}-z_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8}(S_{0},T_{1})}\leqslant\delta_{0}.

Then we have

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,S_{0},z_{0})\xi_{1}(T_{0})+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,S_{0},z_{0})\mathcal{E}_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(I_{0})}\lesssim\tilde{\delta}

and we can apply Lemma
4.12
to obtain

\left\lVert\xi_{0}-\xi_{1}-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,S_{0},z_{0})(\mathcal{E}_{0}-\mathcal{E}_{1})\right\rVert_{[z_{0};S_{0},S_{1};T_{2}]}\leqslant C_{0}\delta_{0}\tilde{\delta}.

Now observe that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}(\cdot,S_{1},z_{0})(\xi_{0}-\xi_{1})(S_{1})+\mathcal{D}(\cdot,S_{1},z_{0})(\mathcal{E}_{0}

\displaystyle-\mathcal{E}_{1})\|_{L^{8}L^{4}([S_{1},T_{1}])}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert z_{0}-z_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{8}(S_{1},T_{1})}\leqslant\delta_{1},

where
\delta_{1}:=\delta_{0}+C_{0}\delta_{0}\tilde{\delta}.
Now if
\delta_{1}\leqslant\tilde{\delta}
, then we repeat this argument to obtain

\left\lVert\xi_{0}-\xi_{1}-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,S_{1},z_{0})(\mathcal{E}_{0}-\mathcal{E}_{1})\right\rVert_{[z_{0};S_{1},S_{2};T_{2}]}\leqslant C_{0}\delta_{1}\tilde{\delta}.

If
\delta_{0}
is sufficiently small then this argument can be repeated up to
I_{N}
. Setting
\delta_{j}=(1+C_{0}\tilde{\delta})^{j}\delta_{0}
, we find

\displaystyle\left\lVert\xi_{0}-\xi_{1}-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,T_{0},z_{0})(\mathcal{E}_{0}-\mathcal{E}_{1})\right\rVert_{[z_{0};T_{0},T_{1};T_{2}]}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{j=0}^{N}\left\lVert\xi_{0}-\xi_{1}-\mathcal{D}(\cdot,S_{j},z_{0})(\mathcal{E}_{0}-\mathcal{E}_{1})\right\rVert_{[z_{0};S_{j},S_{j+1};T_{2}]}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{0}\tilde{\delta}\sum_{j=0}^{N}\delta_{j}=((1+C_{0}\tilde{\delta})^{N+1}-1)\delta_{0}

for sufficiently small
\delta_{0}
.
∎

5.
Linear profile decomposition

In this section, we introduce a linear profile decomposition adapted to the linearized equation

\begin{cases}i\partial_{t}v+Hv=B[z]v,\\
v(s)=\varphi.\end{cases}

Recall that we denote the solution to this equation by
v(t)=\mathcal{L}(t,s;z)
.

We first introduce some notation and a few definitions. For
y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, let
T_{y}
be the translation operator

(T_{y}f)(x)=f(x-y),\quad f:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{C}.

Note that
T_{y}
is not a map from
P_{c}H^{1}
to itself when
y\neq 0
.

Next, we say a sequence
\{(y_{n},s_{n})\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}
is a
parameter of shifts

\quad\text{either}\quad y_{n}\equiv 0\quad\text{or}\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}|y_{n}|=\infty,\quad\text{and}

\quad\text{either}\quad s_{n}\equiv 0\quad\text{or}\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}|s_{n}|=\infty.

Finally, let
\{z_{n}\}\subset\mathrm{SBC}
. We say a sequence of functions
\{\lambda_{n}\}\subset P_{c}H^{1}
is a
linear profile associated with
\{z_{n}\}
if

\lambda_{n}=\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi

for some
\varphi\in H^{1}
and some parameter of shifts

\{(y_{n},s_{n})\}
.

The main result of this section is the following theorem:

Theorem 5.1

(Linear Profile Decomposition)
.

Let
\{z_{n}\}_{n}\subset\mathrm{SBC}
and let
\{\psi_{n}\}_{n}\subset P_{c}H^{1}
be a bounded sequence. Passing to a subsequence, there exists
J^{*}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0,\infty\}
and linear profiles
\{\lambda^{j}_{n}\}_{n}
associated with
\{z_{n}\}_{n}
with shifts
\{(y_{n}^{j},s_{n}^{j})\}_{n}
such that the following decomposition holds for each finite
0\leq J\leq J^{*}
:

\psi_{n}=\sum_{j=0}^{J}\lambda_{n}^{j}+\gamma_{n}^{J},\quad\text{with}\quad\lambda_{n}^{j}:=\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n}^{j};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{j}}\varphi_{\infty}^{j}.

(5.1)

This decomposition satisfies the following properties:

•

The profiles and shift parameters obey the following:

(i)

Writing

y^{j}:=\lim_{n\to\infty}|y^{j}_{n}|\in\{0,\infty\}\quad\text{and}\quad s^{j}:=\lim_{n\to\infty}|s^{j}_{n}|\in\{0,\infty\},

we have
(y^{0},s^{0})=(0,0)
and
(y^{j},s^{j})\neq(0,0)
for
j\geqslant 1
.

(ii)

For each
j\neq k
, we have the following orthogonality of parameters:

\lim_{n\to\infty}\bigl[|s_{n}^{j}-s_{n}^{k}|+|y_{n}^{j}-y_{n}^{k}|\bigr]=\infty.

(iii)

For each
j
,
T_{y_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{j},0;z_{n})\psi_{n}\rightharpoonup\varphi_{\infty}^{j}
weakly in
H^{1}
.

(iv)

We have
\varphi_{\infty}^{j}\neq 0
for
0<j\leqslant J^{*}
and
\varphi_{\infty}^{j}=0
for
j>J^{*}
. Furthermore, if
y^{j}=0
then
\varphi_{\infty}^{j}\in P_{c}H^{1}
.

•

We have the following mass/energy decoupling:

\displaystyle\text{for each }J,\quad\mathbb{M}(\psi_{n})=\sum_{j=0}^{J}\mathbb{M}(\lambda_{n}^{j})+\mathbb{M}(\gamma_{n}^{J})+o(1)\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

(5.2)

\displaystyle\text{for each }J,\quad\mathbb{H}_{0}(\psi_{n})=\sum_{j=0}^{J}\mathbb{H}_{0}(\lambda_{n}^{j})+\mathbb{H}_{0}(\gamma_{n}^{J})+o(1)\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

(5.3)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{G}(\psi_{n})=\mathbb{G}(\varphi^{0}_{\infty})+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{G}(\varphi^{j}_{\infty}){\bf 1}_{\{s^{j}=0\}}.

(5.4)

In addition, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int V(x)|\psi_{n}(x)|^{2}dx=\int V(x)|\varphi_{\infty}^{0}(x)|^{2}dx,

(5.5)

so that

\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{V}(\psi_{n})\geqslant\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}(\varphi^{j}_{\infty};y^{j},s^{j}),

(5.6)

where

\mathbb{E}(\psi;y,s):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathbb{E}_{V}(\psi)&&(y,s)=(0,0),\\
&\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{H}_{0}(\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n}^{j};z_{n})\psi)&&(y,s)=(0,\infty),\\
&\mathbb{E}_{0}(\psi)&&(y,s)=(\infty,0),\\
&\mathbb{H}_{0}(\psi)&&(y,s)=(\infty,\infty).\end{aligned}\right.

•

Finally, the remainder satisfies the following:

\sup_{J}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty,

\lim_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}=0.

(5.7)

Before beginning the proof of Theorem
5.1
, we collect several preliminary results related to the effect of space translations on the linear propagation of the radiation. These results will be used both for the proof of Theorem
5.1
and in later sections.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\{z_{n}\}_{n}\subset\mathrm{SBC}
, and let
\{y_{n}\}
be a sequence such that
|y_{n}|\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
. Then, for any
\varphi\in P_{c}H^{1}
and any
\tau>0
,

T_{y_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\to e^{-it\Delta}\varphi\quad\text{in}\quad\mathrm{Stz}^{1}([-\tau,\tau])\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

Proof.

By a density argument, we may assume that
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}
. We fix
\tau>1
and set

\displaystyle v_{n}(t)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi

for
t\in[-\tau,\tau]
. Observe that

\left\lVert v_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}}\lesssim\left\lVert P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{H^{1}}

(5.8)

uniformly in
n
.

The main step in the proof will be to establish
H^{1}
-smallness for
v_{n}
far from
x=y_{n}
. Combining this with the fact that
|y_{n}|\to\infty
, we can show that the contribution of the potential terms (i.e. the terms with
V
and
B[z_{n}]
) are ultimately negligible. To this end, we let
\chi_{n,r}(x)=\chi(\tfrac{x-y_{n}}{r})
be a smooth function obeying

\chi_{n,r}(x)=\begin{cases}0&|x-y_{n}|<r\\
1&|x-y_{n}|>2r\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\nabla\chi_{n,r}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\tfrac{1}{r}.

Using the equation for
v_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}|v_{n}|^{2}=2\nabla\cdot\operatorname{Im}(\bar{v}_{n}\nabla v_{n})+2\operatorname{Im}(\bar{v}_{n}B[z_{n}]v_{n}),

so that

{\partial}_{t}\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}|^{2}\,dx=-2\int\nabla\chi_{n,R}\cdot\operatorname{Im}(\bar{v}_{n}\nabla v_{n})\,dx+2\int\chi_{n,r}\operatorname{Im}(\bar{v}_{n}B[z_{n}]v_{n})\,dx.

(5.9)

By (
5.8
), we first have

\sup_{t\in[-\tau,\tau]}\left\lvert\int\nabla\chi_{n,r}\cdot\operatorname{Im}(\bar{v}_{n}\nabla v_{n})\,dx\right\rvert\lesssim\tfrac{1}{r}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}.

To estimate the second term, we first note that for each
t\in[-\tau,\tau]
,

R[z_{n}]v_{n}=v_{n}+c_{n}\phi_{0}

for
c_{n}\in\mathbb{C}
obeying

|c_{n}|\lesssim\left\lVert v_{n}D_{1}\Phi[z_{n}]\right\rVert_{L^{1}}+\left\lVert v_{n}D_{2}\Phi[z_{n}]\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\lesssim\left(\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}|^{2}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+o_{n}(1)

for any fixed
r>0
as
n\to\infty
. As
|y_{n}|\to\infty
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert|\Phi[z_{n}]|^{2}R[z_{n}]v_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle{}\leqslant\left\lVert|\Phi[z_{n}]|^{2}\chi_{n,r}R[z_{n}]v_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}+\left\lVert|\Phi[z_{n}]|^{2}(1-\chi_{n,r})R[z_{n}]v_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim\left(\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}|^{2}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+|c_{n}|+\left\lVert|\Phi[z_{n}]|^{2}(1-\chi_{n,r})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim\left(\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}|^{2}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+o_{n}(1)\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty,

which in turn yields

\left\lVert B[z_{n}(t)]v_{n}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left(\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}|^{2}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+o_{n}(1)\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty

for each fixed
r>0
. We thus obtain

\biggl|\partial_{t}\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}(t)|^{2}\,dx\biggr|\lesssim\tfrac{1}{r}+o_{n}(1)+\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}(t)|^{2}\,dx.

(5.10)

We will estimate
{\partial}_{j}v_{n}
for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
in a similar fashion, relying on the equation

i\partial_{t}\partial_{j}v_{n}-\Delta\partial_{j}v_{n}=\partial_{j}[-Vv_{n}+B[z_{n}]v_{n}].

(5.11)

We first claim that

\|v_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{2}}\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}}.

(5.12)

To see this, we use the following Duhamel formula for
v_{n}
:

v_{n}(t)=e^{itH}v_{n}(0)-i\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-s)H}B[z_{n}(s)]v_{n}(s)ds,

which firstly implies

\|Hv_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}\cap L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{6}}\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}}+\|HB[z_{n}]v_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\frac{6}{5}}}.

As we may use the definition of
B[\cdot]
and Proposition
4.6
to obtain

\|HB[z_{n}]v_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\frac{6}{5}}}\lesssim c\|Hv_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{6}}+\|v_{n}\|_{\mathrm{Str}^{1}}\lesssim c\|Hv_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{6}}+\|\varphi\|_{H^{1}}

for some
0<c\ll 1
(depending on the upper bound of
|z_{n}|
), the claim (
5.12
) follows.

We now use (
5.11
) to write

{\partial}_{t}|{\partial}_{j}v_{n}|^{2}=2\nabla\cdot\operatorname{Im}(\overline{{\partial}_{j}v_{n}}\nabla{\partial}_{j}v_{n})+2\operatorname{Im}(\overline{{\partial}_{j}v_{n}}({\partial}_{j}V)v_{n})-2\operatorname{Im}(\overline{{\partial}_{j}v_{n}}{\partial}_{j}(B[z_{n}]v_{n})),

which implies

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}\int\chi_{n,r}|{\partial}_{j}v_{n}|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=-2\int\nabla\chi_{n,r}\cdot\operatorname{Im}(\overline{{\partial}_{j}v_{n}}\nabla{\partial}_{j}v_{n})\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+2\int\chi_{n,r}\operatorname{Im}(\overline{{\partial}_{j}v_{n}}({\partial}_{j}V)v_{n})\,dx

\displaystyle\quad-2\int\chi_{n,r}\operatorname{Im}(\overline{{\partial}_{j}v_{n}}{\partial}_{j}(B[z_{n}]v_{n}))\,dx.

Arguing as above, we find that

\left\lvert{\partial}_{t}\int\chi_{n,r}|{\partial}_{j}v_{n}|^{2}\,dx\right\rvert\lesssim\tfrac{1}{r}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{H^{2}}^{2}+\int\chi_{n,r}|{\partial}_{j}v_{n}|^{2}\,dx+\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}|^{2}\,dx+o_{n}(1).

(5.13)

If we now define

f_{n}(t)=\int\chi_{n,r}\bigl[|v_{n}(t,x)|^{2}+|\nabla v_{n}(t,x)|^{2}\bigr]\,dx,

then (
5.10
) and (
5.13
) imply

f_{n}(t)\leqslant f_{n}(0)+t[\tfrac{C}{r}+o_{n}(1)]+C\int_{0}^{t}f_{n}(s)\,ds

for some constant
C>0
(independent of
t,\tau,r,n
). Thus, Gronwall’s inequality implies

\sup_{t\in[-\tau,\tau]}f_{n}(t)\leqslant e^{C\tau}\left(\int\chi_{n,r}|\varphi|^{2}\,dx+\tau[\tfrac{C}{r}+o_{n}(1)]\right).

In particular, given
\varepsilon>0
and
\tau>0
, if we choose
r=r(\tau,\varepsilon)
sufficiently large, then we obtain that

\sup_{t\in[-\tau,\tau]}\int\chi_{n,r}\bigl[|v_{n}(t,x)|^{2}+|\nabla v_{n}(t,x)|^{2}\bigr]\,dx\leqslant\varepsilon\quad\text{for all}\quad n\quad\text{sufficiently large.}\quad

(5.14)

Using (
5.14
) and the fact that
|y_{n}|\to\infty
, we can therefore deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert Vv_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{t}H^{1}_{x}([-\tau,\tau])}

\displaystyle=0,

(5.15)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert B[z_{n}]v_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{t}H^{1}_{x}([-\tau,\tau])}

\displaystyle=0.

We are now in a position to establish the desired convergence. In particular, our goal is to show that
T_{y_{n}}^{-1}v_{n}(t)\to e^{-it\Delta}\varphi
in
\mathrm{Stz}^{1}([-\tau,\tau])
. We begin with the following Duhamel formula:

T_{y_{n}}^{-1}v_{n}(t)=e^{-it\Delta}T_{y_{n}}^{-1}P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi-i\int_{0}^{t}e^{-i(t-s)\Delta}T_{y_{n}}^{-1}(-Vv_{n}+B[z_{n}]v_{n})(s)\,ds.

(5.16)

We now observe that

\left\lVert T_{y_{n}}^{-1}P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi-\varphi\right\rVert_{H^{1}}=\left\lVert\phi_{0}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}|(\phi_{0},T_{y_{n}}\varphi)|\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

Combining this with Strichartz estimates for the free Schrödinger equation and (
5.15
), we deduce

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert T_{y_{n}}^{-1}v_{n}(t)-e^{-it\Delta}\varphi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1}([-\tau,\tau])}=0.

∎

We use the assumption that
V\in H_{\frac{3}{2}}^{1}
in the following lemma.

Lemma 5.3
.

Suppose that
\{z_{n}\}\subset\mathrm{SBC}
and
\{y_{n}\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
. For any
\varphi\in H^{1}
,

\sup_{n}\|\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\|_{X([0,\infty))}\lesssim\|e^{-it\Delta}\varphi\|_{X([0,\infty))},

(5.17)

where

X\in\{L_{t}^{8}L_{x}^{4}\cap L_{t}^{2}H_{6}^{\frac{1}{2}},L_{t}^{2}H_{6}^{1}\}.

Moreover, the implicit constants are independent of the choice of
z_{n}
. The analogous bounds hold in the negative time direction.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we take space-time norms over
[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. We set

v_{n}(t)=\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\quad\text{and}\quad w(t)=e^{-it\Delta}\varphi,

and we denote

d_{n}(t)=v_{n}(t)-P_{c}T_{y_{n}}w(t),

which satisfies
d_{n}(0)=0
and

(i{\partial}_{t}-H)d_{n}-B[z_{n}]d_{n}=-(i{\partial}_{t}-H)P_{c}T_{y_{n}}w+B[z_{n}](P_{c}T_{y_{n}}w).

Using the equation

\displaystyle-(i{\partial}_{t}-H)P_{c}T_{y_{n}}w

\displaystyle=-(i{\partial}_{t}-H)T_{y_{n}}w+(i{\partial}_{t}-H)\phi_{0}(T_{y_{n}}w,\phi_{0})

\displaystyle=-VT_{y_{n}}w-e_{0}\phi_{0}(T_{y_{n}}w,\phi_{0})+\phi_{0}(T_{y_{n}}i{\partial}_{t}w,\phi_{0})

\displaystyle=-VT_{y_{n}}w-e_{0}\phi_{0}(T_{y_{n}}w,\phi_{0})+\phi_{0}(T_{y_{n}}w,\Delta\phi_{0}),

we obtain the Duhamel formula

\displaystyle d_{n}(t)=-i\int_{0}^{t}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(t,s;z_{n})\bigl\{-VT_{y_{n}}w-e_{0}\phi_{0}(T_{y_{n}}w,\phi_{0})

\displaystyle\quad+\phi_{0}(T_{y_{n}}w,\Delta\phi_{0})+B[z_{n}](P_{c}T_{y_{n}}w)\bigr\}\,ds.

We now turn to the
H^{1/2}
-regularity estimates in (
5.17
). We first observe that

\displaystyle\left\lVert VT_{y_{n}}w\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6/5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert V\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}_{3/2}}\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}}\lesssim\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}},

\displaystyle\left\lVert e_{0}\phi_{0}(T_{y_{n}}w,\phi_{0})\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6/5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert\phi_{0}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}_{6/5}}\left\lVert\phi_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{6/5}}\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{2}L^{6}}\lesssim\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}},

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi_{0}(T_{y_{n}}w,\Delta\phi_{0})\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6/5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}},

\displaystyle\left\lVert B[z_{n}](P_{c}T_{y_{n}}w)\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6/5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert\Phi[z_{n}]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}\left\lVert P_{c}T_{y_{n}}w\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}}\lesssim\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}}.

In particular, by Sobolev and Strichartz, we have

\left\lVert d_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}}\lesssim\left\lVert d_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1/2}}\lesssim\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}}.

Noting that

\left\lVert(1-P_{c})T_{y_{n}}w\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}}\lesssim\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}},

we thus obtain

\left\lVert v_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}}\lesssim\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}},

which yields the first bound in (
5.17
). The other estimate is shown similarly. ∎

The next result pertains to the fact that in general, the operator
\mathcal{L}
is not unitary. Nonetheless, in the regime
|y_{n}|\to\infty
, we can recover the isometry property.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\{z_{n}\}_{n}\subset\mathrm{SBC}
, and suppose
|y_{n}|\to\infty
. Then for any
\varphi\in H^{1}
, we have the following:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left\lvert\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}}-\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=0,

(5.18)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left\lvert\left\lVert\nabla\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}}-\left\lVert\nabla\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=0.

(5.19)

Proof.

We set

v_{n}(t)=\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi

and let
\varepsilon>0
. As
e^{-it\Delta}\varphi\in L^{2}H^{1}_{6}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
(by Strichartz), there exists
\tau>0
such that

\left\lVert e^{-it\Delta}e^{i\tau\Delta}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([0,\infty))}=\left\lVert e^{-it\Delta}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([\tau,\infty))}\leqslant\varepsilon.

(5.20)

Using Lemma
4.4
, the triangle inequality, the uniform Strichartz estimate (Proposition
4.6
), Lemma
5.3
, (
5.20
), and Lemma
5.2
(with the
L_{t}^{\infty}H_{x}^{1}([-\tau,\tau])
norm), we first estimate for all large
n
:

\displaystyle\|v_{n}\|_{L^{2}H_{6}^{1}([\tau,\infty))}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{L}(t,0,z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\|_{L^{2}H_{6}^{1}([\tau,\infty)}

(5.21)

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{L}(t+\tau,0,z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\|_{L^{2}H_{6}^{1}([0,\infty)}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n}(\cdot+\tau))\mathcal{L}(\tau,0;z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\|_{L^{2}H_{6}^{1}([0,\infty))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n}(\cdot+\tau))P_{c}T_{y_{n}}e^{i\tau\Delta}\phi\|_{L^{2}H_{6}^{1}([0,\infty))}

\displaystyle+\|\mathcal{L}(\tau,0;z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi-T_{y_{n}}e^{i\tau\Delta}\varphi\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|e^{-it\Delta}e^{i\tau\Delta}\phi\|_{L^{2}H_{6}^{1}([0,\infty))}+\varepsilon\lesssim\varepsilon.

Next, we observe that we have the following Duhamel formula for
v_{n}
:

v_{n}(t)=e^{i(t-\tau)\Delta}v_{n}(\tau)-i\int_{\tau}^{t}e^{-i(t-s)\Delta}(-Vv_{n}+B[z_{n}]v_{n})\,ds.

Thus for
k\in\{0,1\}
Strichartz and (
5.21
) imply

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle v_{n}(t)-e^{i(t-\tau)\Delta}v_{n}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}\dot{H}^{k}([\tau,\infty))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert Vv_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}{H}^{1}_{6/5}([\tau,\infty))}+\left\lVert B[z_{n}]v_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6/5}([\tau,\infty))}\lesssim\left\lVert v_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}([\tau,\infty))}\lesssim\varepsilon.

As
e^{it\Delta}
is unitary on
\dot{H}^{k}
, this implies

\sup_{t\geqslant\tau}\left\lvert\left\lVert v_{n}(t)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{k}}-\left\lVert v_{n}(\tau)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{k}}\right\rvert\lesssim\varepsilon

for all large
n
. Thus the result follows provided we can establish

\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}\left\lvert\|v_{n}(s)\|_{\dot{H}^{k}}-\|\varphi\|_{\dot{H}^{k}}\right\rvert\lesssim\varepsilon

for all large
n
. In fact, this follows from Lemma
5.2
and the unitary property of the free propagator and space translation; indeed, we have

\displaystyle\|v_{n}(s)-T_{y_{n}}e^{-is\Delta}\varphi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{1}([0,\tau])}\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

As similar estimates hold in the negative time direction, we complete the proof. ∎

We next prove a result that collects the ways in which we may consider two parameters of shifts to be ‘orthogonal’.

Lemma 5.5

(Asymptotic orthogonality)
.

Let
\{z_{n}\}_{n}\subset\mathrm{SBC}
, and let
\{(y_{n},s_{n})\}_{n}
and
\{(\tilde{y}_{n},\tilde{s}_{n})\}_{n}
be two parameters of shifts.

The following are equivalent:

(i)

|s_{n}-\tilde{s}_{n}|+|y_{n}-\tilde{y}_{n}|\to\infty

as
n\to\infty
;

(ii)

For any
\varphi\in H^{1}

T_{\tilde{y}_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}_{n},s_{n};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty

weakly in
H^{1}
up to a subsequence.

(iii)

For any subsequence
\{n_{k}\}_{k}
, there exists a bounded sequence
\{u_{k}\}_{k}\subset P_{c}H^{1}
and a subsequence in
k
such that

T_{y_{n_{k}}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n_{k}},0;z_{n_{k}})u_{k}\rightharpoonup\psi\neq 0\quad\text{and}\quad T_{\tilde{y}_{n_{k}}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}_{n_{k}},0;z_{n_{k}})u_{k}\rightharpoonup 0

weakly in
H^{1}
as
k\to\infty
along this subsequence.

We first need the following:

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\{z_{n}\}_{n}\subset\mathrm{SBC}
and suppose that two parameters of shifts
\{(y_{n},s_{n})\}_{n}
and
\{(\tilde{y}_{n},\tilde{s}_{n})\}_{n}
satisfy

\sup_{n}(|s_{n}-\tilde{s_{n}}|+|y_{n}-\tilde{y}_{n}|)<\infty.

Then, up to a subsequence, the sequence of operators

T_{\tilde{y}_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}_{n},s_{n};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}

converges strongly to a continuous invertible operator on
H^{1}
.

Proof.

If
y_{n}\equiv 0
, the theorem follows from the local-in-time convergence of
z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n})
(cf.
[
19
, Equation (5.13)]
). Thus we suppose instead that
|y_{n}|\to\infty
. Passing to a subsequence, we may suppose
s_{n}-\tilde{s}_{n}\to s_{\infty}\in\mathbb{R}
and
y_{n}-\tilde{y}_{n}\to y_{\infty}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Without loss of generality, we may then assume
y_{\infty}=0
and
y_{n}\equiv\tilde{y}_{n}
. Under these conditions, Lemma
5.2
implies that for any
\varphi\in H^{1}
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}T_{{y}_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}_{n}-s_{n},0;z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}))P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi=e^{is_{\infty}\Delta}\varphi,

where the limit is taken strongly in
H^{1}
. This implies the result.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.5
.

We will show that (i)
\implies
(ii)
\implies
(iii)
\implies
(i).

(i)
\Rightarrow
(ii):
By a density argument, we may assume
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}
. Passing to a subsequence, we may suppose that either

\lim_{n\to\infty}|\tilde{s}_{n}-s_{n}|=\infty\quad\text{ or }\quad\sup_{n}|\tilde{s}_{n}-s_{n}|<\infty.

If
|s_{n}-\tilde{s}_{n}|\to\infty
, then the dispersive estimate of Lemma
4.10
and the fact that
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}
implies

\|\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}_{n},s_{n};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\|_{L^{4}+L^{8}}\lesssim|\tilde{s}_{n}-s_{n}|^{-\frac{3}{4}}\|\varphi\|_{L^{4/3}\cap L^{8/7}}\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty,

which implies the result.

If instead
|s_{n}-\tilde{s}_{n}|
is bounded, then we must have
|y_{n}-\tilde{y}_{n}|\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
. In this case, Lemma
5.6
implies

\varphi_{n}:=T_{y_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}_{n},s_{n};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\to\varphi_{\infty}

strongly in
H^{1}
along a subsequence. Thus

|y_{n}-\tilde{y}_{n}|\to\infty\implies T_{y_{n}-\tilde{y}_{n}}\varphi_{n}\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{weakly in }H^{1}\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

(ii)
\Rightarrow
(iii):
Assuming (ii), item (iii) follows with the choice

u_{k}=\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n_{k}};z_{n_{k}})P_{c}T_{y_{n_{k}}}\psi\in P_{c}H^{1}

for some nonzero
\psi\in P_{c}H^{1}
.

(iii)
\Rightarrow
(i):
Suppose (iii) holds but (i) fails, so that in particular

\sup_{n}(|s_{n}-\tilde{s_{n}}|+|y_{n}-\tilde{y}_{n}|)<\infty.

By Lemma
5.6
, one may choose a subsequence
\{n_{k}\}_{k}
such that

D_{k}:=T_{\tilde{y}_{n_{k}}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}_{n_{k}},s_{n_{k}};z_{n_{k}})P_{c}T_{y_{n_{k}}}

converges strongly to a continuous invertible operator on
H^{1}
, say
D_{\infty}.
Using (iii), we can find a bounded sequence
\{u_{k}\}_{k}\subset P_{c}H^{1}
and a subsequence in
k
such that

T_{y_{n_{k}}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n_{k}},0;z_{n_{k}})u_{k}\rightharpoonup\psi\neq 0\quad\text{and}\quad T_{\tilde{y}_{n_{k}}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}_{n_{k}},0;z_{n_{k}})u_{k}\rightharpoonup 0.

Now fix
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}
. Then

(T_{\tilde{y}_{n_{k}}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}_{n_{k}},0;z_{n_{k}})u_{k},\varphi)\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad k\to\infty,

while

(T_{\tilde{y}_{n_{k}}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}_{n_{k}},0;z_{n_{k}})u_{k},\varphi)=(D_{k}T_{y_{n_{k}}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n_{k}},0;z_{n_{k}})u_{k},\varphi)\to(D_{\infty}\psi,\varphi)\quad\text{as}\quad k\to\infty.

As
\varphi
was arbitrary and
D_{\infty}
is invertible, we deduce that
\psi=0
, a contradiction. ∎

The next lemma is again related to the lack of unitarity of
\mathcal{L}
. In particular, this lemma is needed to establish a vanishing property for orthogonal profiles.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
\{z_{n}\}\subset\mathrm{SBC}
. For any parameter of shifts
(s_{n},y_{n})
, the following sequence of operators converges strongly along a subsequence:

T_{y_{n}}^{-1}e^{is_{n}H}\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}:H^{1}\to H^{1}.

Furthermore, if
|y_{n}|\to\infty
, then then the limit is identity.

Proof.

If
s_{n}\equiv 0
, then the result is straightforward. We will therefore focus on the case
|s_{n}|\to\infty
and
|y_{n}|\to\infty
(and note that the case
|s_{n}|\to\infty
and
y_{n}\equiv 0
may be handled similarly, cf.
[
19
, Lemma 5.2]
).

We first claim that

T_{y_{n}}^{-1}e^{-is_{n}\Delta}\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\to\text{Id}\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty

(5.22)

strongly as an operator on
H^{1}
. To see this, first let
\varphi\in H^{1}
and set

v_{n}(t)=\mathcal{L}(t,s_{n};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi.

As in (
5.16
), we may write

v_{n}(0)=e^{is_{n}\Delta}v_{n}(s_{n})-i\int_{s_{n}}^{0}e^{is\Delta}(-Vv_{n}+B[z_{n}]v_{n})(s)\,ds,

so that

T_{y_{n}}^{-1}e^{-is_{n}\Delta}v_{n}(0)=T_{y_{n}}^{-1}P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi-i\int_{s_{n}}^{0}e^{-i(s_{n}-s)\Delta}T_{y_{n}}^{-1}(-Vv_{n}+B[z_{n}]v_{n})(s)\,ds.

(5.23)

We now show that the right-hand side converges in
H^{1}
. We have seen before that

T_{y_{n}}^{-1}P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\to\varphi\quad\text{strongly in}\quad H^{1},

so we turn to the second term and establish convergence to zero.

We suppose that
s_{n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
(the case
s_{n}\to-\infty
is handled similarly). We let
\varepsilon>0
and fix
\tau_{0}\geqslant 0
to be chosen below. For large
n
, we then have
s_{n}\geqslant\tau_{0}
and thus, by Strichartz,

\displaystyle\biggl\|\int_{s_{n}-\tau_{0}}^{0}

\displaystyle e^{-i(s_{n}-s)\Delta}T_{y_{n}}^{-1}(-Vv_{n}+B[z_{n}]v_{n})(s)\,ds\biggr\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup_{t\leqslant-\tau_{0}}\left\lVert\int^{-\tau_{0}}_{t}e^{is\Delta}T_{y_{n}}^{-1}(-Vv_{n}+B[z_{n}(s+s_{n})]v_{n})(s+s_{n})\,ds\right\rVert_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert T_{y_{n}}^{-1}(-Vv_{n}+B[z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n})]v_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}))\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6/5}((-\infty,-\tau_{0}])}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert v_{n}(s_{n}+\cdot)\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}((-\infty,-\tau_{0}])}.

By (
5.17
), Lemma
4.4
, Strichartz, and Lemma
5.2
, we have

\displaystyle\|v_{n}

\displaystyle(s_{n}+\cdot)\|_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}((-\infty,-\tau_{0}])}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,-\tau_{0};z_{n}(\cdot+\tau_{0}+s_{n}))P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}((-\infty,0])}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert e^{-it\Delta}T_{y_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(0,-\tau_{0};z_{n}(\cdot+\tau_{0}+s_{n}))P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}((-\infty,0])}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert e^{-it\Delta}e^{i\tau_{0}\Delta}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}((-\infty,0])}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert T_{y_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(0,-\tau_{0};z_{n}(\cdot+\tau_{0}+s_{n}))P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi-e^{i\tau_{0}\Delta}\varphi\right\rVert_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert e^{-it\Delta}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{1}_{6}((-\infty,-\tau_{0}])}+o_{n}(1)\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

Thus, choosing
\tau_{0}
sufficiently large, we find that for all large
n
,

\left\lVert\int_{s_{n}-\tau_{0}}^{0}e^{-i(s_{n}-s)\Delta}T_{y_{n}}^{-1}(-Vv_{n}+B[z_{n}]v_{n})(s)\,ds\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leqslant\varepsilon

On the other hand, we may write

\displaystyle\int_{s_{n}}^{s_{n}-\tau_{0}}

\displaystyle e^{-i(s_{n}-s)\Delta}T_{y_{n}}^{-1}(-Vv_{n}+B[z_{n}]v_{n})(s)\,ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{-\tau_{0}}e^{is\Delta}T_{y_{n}}^{-1}(-Vv_{n}+B[z_{n}]v_{n})(s+s_{n})\,ds

Now, Lemma
5.2
, we have that

\|v_{n}(s+s_{n})-T_{y_{n}}e^{-it\Delta}\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}((-\tau_{0},0))}\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

Thus, since

\int_{0}^{-\tau_{0}}e^{is\Delta}T_{y_{n}}^{-1}(-VT_{y_{n}}e^{-is\Delta}\varphi+B[z_{n}(s+s_{n})]T_{y_{n}}e^{-is\Delta}\varphi)\,ds\to 0\quad\text{in}\quad H^{1},

we deduce

\int_{s_{n}}^{s_{n}-\tau_{0}}e^{-i(s_{n}-s)\Delta}T_{y_{n}}^{-1}(-Vv_{n}+B[z_{n}]v_{n})(s)ds\to 0\quad\text{in}\quad H^{1}\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

Returning to (
5.23
), we conclude that

-i\int_{s_{n}}^{0}e^{-i(s_{n}-s)\Delta}T_{y_{n}}^{-1}(-Vv_{n}+B[z_{n}]v_{n})(s)ds\to 0

in
H^{1}
as
n\to\infty
, which proves the claim (
5.22
).

It remains to upgrade from
e^{-is_{n}\Delta}
to
e^{is_{n}H}
in (
5.22
). First, using (
5.22
) with the choice
z_{n}\equiv 0
, we find that

T_{y_{n}}^{-1}e^{-is_{n}\Delta}e^{-is_{n}H}P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\to\text{Id}\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty

(as operators on
H^{1}
). As
e^{-itH}
is unitary, this also implies the convergence of the adjoint, that is:

T_{y_{n}}^{-1}e^{is_{n}H}P_{c}e^{is_{n}\Delta}T_{y_{n}}\to\text{Id}\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

Thus,

\displaystyle T_{y_{n}}^{-1}

\displaystyle e^{is_{n}H}\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}

\displaystyle=(T_{y_{n}}^{-1}e^{is_{n}H}P_{c}e^{is_{n}\Delta}T_{y_{n}})(T_{y_{n}}^{-1}e^{-is_{n}\Delta}\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}})\to\text{Id}

as
n\to\infty
.∎

With Lemma
5.7
in place, we can prove the following consequence of orthogonality.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
\{z_{n}\}_{n}\subset\mathrm{SBC}
, and let

\lambda_{n}^{j}=\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n}^{j};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{j}}\varphi^{j}\quad\text{and}\quad\lambda_{n}^{k}=\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n}^{k};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{k}}\varphi^{k}

be two linear profiles. If the parameters of shifts
(s_{n}^{j},y_{n}^{j})
and
(s_{n}^{k},y_{n}^{k})
obey

|s_{n}^{j}-s_{n}^{k}|+|y_{n}^{j}-y_{n}^{k}|\to\infty\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty,

then

|(\lambda_{n}^{j},\lambda_{n}^{k})|+|(H\lambda_{n}^{j},\lambda_{n}^{k})|\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

Furthermore, if
\{r_{n}\}
is a bounded sequence in
H^{1}
which satisfies

T_{y_{n}^{j}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{j},0;z_{n})r_{n}\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{weakly in}\quad H^{1},

then

|(\lambda_{n}^{j},r_{n})|+|(H\lambda_{n}^{j},r_{n})|\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

Proof.

We focus on the
(H\cdot,\cdot)
inner product.

We suppose
|s_{n}^{j}-s_{n}^{k}|\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
. As
e^{itH}
is unitary and commutes with
H
, we may write

\displaystyle(H\lambda_{n}^{j},\lambda_{n}^{k})=((-\Delta+(T_{y_{n}^{j}}^{-1}V))A_{n}\varphi^{j},A_{n}C_{n}\varphi^{k}),

where

A_{n}:=T_{y_{n}^{j}}^{-1}e^{is_{n}^{j}H}\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n}^{j};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{j}}\quad\text{and}\quad C_{n}:=T_{y_{n}^{j}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{j},s_{n}^{k};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{k}}.

By Lemma
5.7
, we have that
A_{n}
converges strongly (as operators on
H^{1}
). Moreover, by Lemma
5.5
,
C_{n}\varphi^{k}\rightharpoonup 0
weakly in
H^{1}
. Thus

((-\Delta)A_{n}\varphi^{j},e^{is_{n}H}A_{n}C_{n}\varphi^{k})\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

As
|s_{n}^{j}-s_{n}^{k}|\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, we also see that
C_{n}\to 0
strongly as an operator
H^{1}\to L^{4}
, which yields

((T_{y_{n}^{j}}^{-1}V))A_{n}\varphi^{j},A_{n}C_{n}\varphi^{k})\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

We next suppose that
|s_{n}^{j}-s_{n}^{k}|
is bounded, which necessarily implies
|y_{n}^{j}|+|y_{n}^{k}|\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
. Without loss of generality, we suppose that
|y_{n}^{k}|\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
. Choosing a subsequence along which
s_{n}^{j}-s_{n}^{k}\to\tilde{s}_{\infty}
, Lemma
5.2
and Lemma
5.7
together yield

\displaystyle T_{y_{n}^{k}}^{-1}T_{y_{n}^{j}}A_{n}C_{n}

\displaystyle=(T_{y_{n}^{k}}^{-1}e^{is_{n}^{j}H}\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n}^{j};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{k}})(T_{y_{n}^{k}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{j},s_{n}^{k};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{k}})\to e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\infty}\Delta}

strongly in
H^{1}
. Combining this with the convergence of
A_{n}
, the problem reduces to showing that

((-\Delta+(T_{y_{n}^{j}}^{-1}V))A\varphi^{j},T_{y_{n}^{k}-y_{n}^{j}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\infty}\Delta}\varphi^{k})\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

In fact, this convergence is straightforward, as both

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}((-\Delta)A\varphi^{j},T_{y_{n}^{k}-y_{n}^{j}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\infty}\Delta}\varphi^{k})=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert(A\varphi^{j})(T_{y_{n}^{k}-y_{n}^{j}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\infty}\Delta}\varphi^{k})\right\rVert_{L^{1}\cap L^{2}}=0,

follow from the fact that
\lim_{n\to\infty}|y_{n}^{j}-y_{n}^{k}|=\infty
.

We turn to the other estimate. We let

\tilde{r}_{n}=T_{y_{n}^{j}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{j},0;z_{n})r_{n}.

Using Lemma
5.7
, it suffices to show that

((-\Delta+(T_{y_{n}^{j}}^{-1}V))A\varphi^{j},{A^{*}}\tilde{r}_{n})\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty,

where

A:=\lim_{n\to\infty}T_{y_{n}^{j}}^{-1}e^{is_{n}^{j}H}\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n}^{j};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{j}}

(along some subsequence). The convergence

\lim_{n\to\infty}(-\Delta A\varphi^{j},{A^{*}}\tilde{r}_{n})=0

is immediate from the weak convergence of
\tilde{r}_{n}
. Let us therefore consider the potential term. As
A\tilde{r}_{n}
converges to zero weakly in
H^{1}
, we have

\sup_{R>0}\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leqslant R\}}A\tilde{r}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{4}}=0.

Then, given
\varepsilon>0
, we choose
R_{0}>0
such that

\left\lVert{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\geqslant R_{0}\}}A\varphi^{j}\right\rVert\leqslant\varepsilon

and estimate

\displaystyle\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}|((T_{y_{n}^{j}}^{-1}V)A\varphi^{j},A\tilde{r}_{n})|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert V\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\geqslant R_{0}\}}A\varphi^{j}\right\rVert_{L^{4}}\sup_{n}\left\lVert A\tilde{r}_{n}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert V\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert A\varphi^{j}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leqslant R_{0}\}}A\tilde{r}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon.

As
\varepsilon
was arbitrary, we obtain the result.
∎

We turn finally to the construction of the linear profile decomposition. The main step is to show that if
\psi_{n}
is a bounded sequence in
H^{1}
with the property that
\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\psi_{n}
is nontrivial in
L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{4}
, then we can extract a ‘bubble of concentration’. To achieve this, we proceed essentially as in
[
8
]
(see also
[
2
,
4
,
16
]
).

Definition 5.9
.

Given
\{z_{n}\}_{n}\subset\mathrm{SBC}
and a bounded sequence
\{\psi_{n}\}_{n}\subset P_{c}H^{1}
, we let
\mathcal{V}(\{z_{n}\},\{\psi_{n}\})
denote the set of all
\varphi\in H^{1}
such that there exist parameters of shifts
(s_{n},y_{n})
and a sequence in
n
along which

T_{y_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n},0;z_{n})\psi_{n}\rightharpoonup\varphi\quad\text{weakly in}\quad H^{1}.

We set

\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\psi_{n}\})=\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{V}(\{z_{n}\},\{\psi_{n}\})}\|\varphi\|_{H^{1}}.

Remark 5.10
.

In the non-radial setting, space translation must be taken into account. Because of the presence of translation, the elements of
\mathcal{V}
do not necessarily belong to
P_{c}H^{1}
.

Proposition 5.11

(Control on vanishing)
.

Let
\{z_{n}\}_{n}\subset\mathrm{SBC}
and let
\{\psi_{n}\}_{n}\subset P_{c}H^{1}
be a bounded sequence.
If

\sup_{n}\left\lVert\psi_{n}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leqslant M\quad\text{and}\quad\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\psi_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R},L^{4}_{x})}\geqslant\varepsilon,

then there exists
\beta=\beta(M,\varepsilon)>0
such that

\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\psi_{n}\})\geqslant\beta.

Proof.

Extracting a subsequence if necessary, we may suppose that

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\psi_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R},L^{4}_{x})}\geqslant\varepsilon/2\quad\text{for all}\quad n.

First, by Sobolev embedding,

\left\lVert P_{\geqslant N}\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\psi_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R},L^{4}_{x})}\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{4}}\left\lVert P_{\geqslant N}\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\psi_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R},H^{1})}\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{4}}M.

Thus there exists
N=N(M,\varepsilon)
such that

\left\lVert P_{<N}\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\psi_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R},L^{4}_{x})}\geqslant\varepsilon/4\quad\text{for all}\quad n.

On the other hand, by Hölder’s inequality,

\left\lVert P_{<N}\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\psi_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R},L^{4}_{x})}\lesssim M^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert P_{<N}\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\psi_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R},L^{\infty}_{x})}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

It follows that

\left\lVert P_{<N}\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\psi_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R},L^{\infty}_{x})}\gtrsim\varepsilon^{2}/M\quad\text{for all}\quad n,

and thus there exist
(s_{n},y_{n})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that

|(P_{<N}\mathcal{L}(s_{n},0;z_{n})\psi_{n})(y_{n})|\gtrsim\varepsilon^{2}/M.

We now observe that

|(P_{<N}\mathcal{L}(s_{n},0;z_{n})\psi_{n})(y_{n})|=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}g(y)(\mathcal{L}(s_{n},0;z_{n})\psi_{n})(y+y_{n})\,dy\right|

for some Schwartz kernel
g
. As
\{T^{-1}_{y_{n}}\mathcal{L}(s_{n},0;z_{n})\psi_{n}\}_{n}
is a bounded sequence in
H^{1}
, it converges weakly along a subsequence to some
\varphi\in H^{1}
. Along this subsequence, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}g(y)(\mathcal{L}(s_{n},0;z_{n})\psi_{n})(y+y_{n})\,dy\to\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}g(y)\varphi(y)\,dy,

which implies

\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\gtrsim\varepsilon^{2}/M.

Now, if
(s_{n},y_{n})
is already a parameter of shifts (so that
s_{n}\equiv 0
or
|s_{n}|\to\infty
and
y_{n}\equiv 0
or
|y_{n}|\to\infty
), then we obtain that
\varphi\in\mathcal{V}(\{z_{n}\},\{\psi_{n}\})
and

\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\psi_{n}\})\geq\|\varphi\|_{H^{1}}\gtrsim\varepsilon^{2}/M,

and the proof is complete. Otherwise, we argue as follows.

If
\{s_{n}\}
and
\{y_{n}\}
are both bounded, then we may pass to a subsequence in
n
so that
s_{n}\to s_{0}
,
y_{n}\to y_{0}
, and
z_{n}\to z_{\infty}
(locally uniformly). Then the convergence

T_{y_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n},0;z_{n})\psi_{n}\rightharpoonup\varphi

yields

(\psi_{n},h)=(\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}(T_{y_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n},0;z_{n})\psi_{n}),h)\to(\mathcal{L}(0,s_{0};z_{\infty})P_{c}T_{y_{0}}\varphi,h)

as
n\to\infty
for all
h\in H^{1}
. This reads as

\mathcal{L}(0,0;z_{n})\psi_{n}=\psi_{n}\rightharpoonup\tilde{\varphi}:=(\mathcal{L}(0,s_{0};z_{\infty})P_{c}T_{y_{0}})^{*}\varphi\in\mathcal{V}(\{z_{n}\},\{\psi_{n}\}),

with

\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\psi_{n}\})\geq\|\tilde{\varphi}\|_{H^{1}}\gtrsim\|\varphi\|_{H^{1}}\gtrsim\varepsilon^{2}/M,

as desired. Similar arguments treat the case that only one of the sequences
\{s_{n}\},\{y_{n}\}
is bounded. ∎

We turn to the proof of Theorem
5.1
. We follow the standard argument, removing one bubble of concentration at a time.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Passing to a subsequence, we first find
\varphi_{\infty}^{0}\in H^{1}
so that
\psi_{n}\rightharpoonup\varphi_{\infty}^{0}
weakly in
H^{1}
. We then define
\lambda^{0}_{n}=\varphi_{\infty}^{0}
and
\gamma_{n}^{0}=\psi_{n}-\varphi_{\infty}^{0}
, so that (
5.1
) holds for
J=0
. Moreover,

\gamma_{n}^{0}\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{weakly in }H^{1}\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

(5.24)

Now, if
\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\gamma_{n}^{0}\})=0
, then the result holds with
J^{*}=0
. In this case, we let
\varphi_{\infty}^{j}=0
for all
j\geqslant 1
and pick arbitrary
\{(y_{n}^{j},s_{n}^{j})\}
for
j\geqslant 1
so that the orthogonality condition is fulfilled.

Suppose instead that
\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\gamma_{n}^{0}\})>0
. Then, by definition, there exists
\varphi_{\infty}^{1}\in\mathcal{V}(\{z_{n}\},\{\psi_{n}\})
such that

\left\lVert\varphi_{\infty}^{1}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\geqslant\tfrac{1}{2}\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\gamma_{n}^{0}\})>0

and a parameter of shifts
\{(y_{n}^{1},s_{n}^{1})\}
such that

T^{-1}_{y_{n}^{1}}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{1},0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{0}\rightharpoonup\varphi_{\infty}^{1}\quad\text{weakly in }H^{1}

(5.25)

as
n\to\infty
along a subsequence.

We now set

\lambda^{1}_{n}:=\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n}^{1};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{1}}\varphi_{\infty}^{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma^{1}_{n}:=\psi_{n}-\lambda_{n}^{1},

which yields the decomposition (
5.1
) for
J=1
. In view of Lemma
5.5
, we see from (
5.24
) and (
5.25
) that the property (ii) holds for
(j,k)=(0,1)
. Moreover, by (
5.25
)
and the definition of
\gamma_{n}^{1}
and
\lambda_{n}^{1}
, we have

T^{-1}_{y_{n}^{1}}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{1},0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{1}\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{weakly in }H^{1}\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

(5.26)

Furthermore, by Lemma
5.5
(ii), Proposition
5.8
, and (
5.1
), we have

\left\lVert\gamma_{n}^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=\left\lVert\lambda_{n}^{1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\lVert\gamma_{n}^{1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+o(1)\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty,

(5.27)

\mathbb{H}_{V}(\gamma_{n}^{0})=\mathbb{H}_{V}(\lambda_{n}^{1})+\mathbb{H}_{V}(\gamma_{n}^{1})+o(1)\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

(5.28)

If
\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\gamma_{n}^{1}\})=0
, the result holds with
J^{*}=1
. We obtain the result by defining the remaining profiles suitably (as in the case of
J^{*}=0
). If instead
\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\gamma_{n}^{1}\})>0
, then there exists
\varphi_{\infty}^{2}\in\mathcal{V}(\{z_{n}\},\{\gamma_{n}^{1}\})
such that

\left\lVert\varphi_{\infty}^{2}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\geqslant\tfrac{1}{2}\eta(\{z_{n}\}_{n},\{\gamma_{n}^{1}\}_{n})>0.

Moreover, there exist
\{(y_{n}^{2},s_{n}^{2})\}_{n}
such that

T^{-1}_{y_{n}^{2}}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{2},0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{1}\rightharpoonup\varphi_{\infty}^{2}\quad\text{weakly in }H^{1}

(5.29)

as
n\to\infty
along a subsequence. We then set

\lambda^{2}_{n}:=\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n}^{2};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{2}}\varphi_{\infty}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma^{2}_{n}:=\gamma^{2}_{n}-\lambda_{n}^{2},

which yields the decomposition (
5.1
) for
J=2
. Using Lemma
5.5
, we see from (
5.26
) and (
5.29
) that the property (ii) holds for
(j,k)=(1,2)
. By (
5.29
) and the definition of
\gamma_{n}^{2}
and
\lambda_{n}^{2}
, we also have

T^{-1}_{y_{n}^{2}}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{2},0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{2}\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{weakly in }H^{1}\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

(5.30)

We repeat this procedure and define
\varphi_{\infty}^{m}
,
\{(y_{n}^{m},s_{n}^{m})\}
, and
\gamma_{n}^{m}
inductively as long as
\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\gamma_{n}^{m-1}\})>0
. Indeed, if
\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\gamma_{n}^{m-1}\})>0
then one finds
\varphi^{m}_{\infty}\in H^{1}
so that

\left\lVert\varphi_{\infty}^{m}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\geqslant\tfrac{1}{2}\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\gamma_{n}^{m-1}\})>0.

(5.31)

We define
(y_{n}^{m},s_{n}^{m})
so that

T^{-1}_{y_{n}^{m}}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{m},0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{m-1}\rightharpoonup\varphi_{\infty}^{m}\quad\text{weakly in }H^{1}

(5.32)

We then derive the decomposition (
5.1
) with
J=m
from the decomposition with
J=m-1
by defining
\gamma_{n}^{m}
via
\gamma_{n}^{m-1}=\lambda_{n}^{m}+\gamma_{n}^{m}
. In this case we obtain

T^{-1}_{y_{n}^{m}}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{m},0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{m}\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{weakly in }H^{1}\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

(5.33)

Using Lemma
5.5
as above, we see from (
5.32
) and (
5.33
) that property (ii) holds for
(j,k)=(m-1,m)
. Moreover, by Proposition
5.8
and (
5.33
), we have

\left\lVert\gamma^{m-1}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=\left\lVert\lambda_{n}^{m}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\lVert\gamma_{n}^{m}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+o(1)\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty,

(5.34)

\mathbb{H}(\gamma^{m-1}_{n})=\mathbb{H}(\lambda_{n}^{m})+\mathbb{H}(\gamma_{n}^{m})+o(1)\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

(5.35)

If
\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\gamma_{n}^{J_{0}}\})=0
holds for some
J_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
then we let
J^{*}=J_{0}
and define the linear profiles for
j\geqslant J^{*}
suitably as above. Otherwise,
J^{*}=\infty
.

It remains to check the desired properties of the decomposition. By induction on
j-k
, we see that

T^{-1}_{y_{n}^{k}}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{k},0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{j}\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{weakly in }H^{1}

(5.36)

and that the property (ii) holds for all
0\leqslant k\leqslant j\leqslant J^{*}
. The property (i) follows from (ii) and the definition of
(y_{n}^{0},s_{n}^{0})
. The property (iii) follows from (
5.1
) for
J=j-1
, (
5.32
), the property (ii), and Lemma
5.5
. Finally, the property (iv) was shown in (
5.31
) and the definition of
J^{*}
.

We now consider the first several decoupling statements. The decomposition (
5.2
) follows from (
5.27
) and (
5.34
). Next, we see from (
5.28
) and (
5.35
) that

\mathbb{H}_{V}(\psi_{n})=\sum_{j=0}^{J}\mathbb{H}_{V}(\lambda_{n}^{j})+\mathbb{H}_{V}(\gamma_{n}^{J})+o_{n}(1).

(5.37)

Now, if
\{v_{n}\}
is a bounded sequence in
H^{1}
which converges to zero weakly in
H^{1}
as
n\to\infty
, then one sees from

\sup_{R>0}\left\lVert v_{n}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leqslant R\}}\right\rVert_{L^{4}}=0

and the fact that
V\in L^{2}
that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int V|v_{n}|^{2}dx=\lim_{n\to\infty}\int Vv_{n}\overline{w}dx=0

for any
w\in H^{1}
. As
(y^{j},s^{j})\neq(0,0)
for all
j\geqslant 1
, we see that

\lambda_{n}^{j}\rightharpoonup 0
weakly in
H^{1}
as
n\to\infty
for all
j\geqslant 1
. Moreover, we see from (
5.1
) that
\gamma_{n}^{j}\rightharpoonup 0
weakly in
H^{1}
as
n\to\infty
for all
j\geqslant 1
. Thus, together with (
5.1
), one has

\int V|\lambda_{n}^{j}|^{2}dx=o_{n}(1)\quad\text{and}\quad\int V|\gamma_{n}^{j}|^{2}dx=o_{n}(1)\quad\text{for all}\quad j\geqslant 1,

(5.38)

along with the desired equality (
5.5
). Combining (
5.38
) and (
5.5
) with (
5.37
), one then obtains (
5.3
).

We next establish the desired bounds on the remainder. The uniform bound is an immediate consequence of the uniform Strichartz estimate (Proposition
4.6
), so we turn to (
5.7
). We only consider the case
J^{*}=\infty
. In this case, we can use (
5.2
), (
5.3
), and
\lVert\lambda_{n}^{j}\rVert_{H^{1}}\sim\lVert\varphi_{\infty}^{j}\rVert_{H^{1}}
to obtain

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left\lVert\varphi_{\infty}^{j}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\sup_{n}\left\lVert\psi_{n}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}<\infty.

Thus,
0<\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\gamma_{n}^{J}\})<2\left\lVert\varphi_{\infty}^{J}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\to 0
as
J\to\infty
. By Proposition
5.11
, this implies (
5.7
).

Finally, we address the remaining decoupling statements. First, we deduce (
5.4
) from (
5.7
) as in the proof of (
2.13
), relying on the fact that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{G}(\lambda_{n}^{j})=0

if
s^{j}=\infty
. Finally, combining (
5.3
), (
5.4
), (
5.5
), and (
5.19
), we obtain (
5.6
). ∎

Finally, we observe that by a suitable modification of the profiles (as in the last paragraph of the proof of Proposition
5.11
), we may choose the time-translation parameters so that for each
j,k
, we have either

\lim_{n\to\infty}|s_{n}^{j}-s_{n}^{k}|=\infty\quad\text{or}\quad s_{n}^{j}\equiv s_{n}^{k}.

We will work under this assumption in what follows.

6.
Proof of Proposition
3.7

In this section we prove the key convergence result, Proposition
3.7
. We first write the solutions
u_{n}
as

u_{n}=\Phi[z_{n}]+R[z_{n}]\xi_{n}

We then have
\{z_{n}\}\subset\mathrm{SBC}
by mass conservation and
a priori

H^{1}
bounds. We apply the linear profile decomposition (Theorem
5.1
) to the bounded sequence
\{\xi_{n}(0)\}_{n}\subset P_{c}H^{1}
. In particular, up to a subsequence in
n
, we obtain
J^{*}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0,\infty\}
, sequences

(s_{n}^{j},y_{n}^{j})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\quad\text{and}\quad\varphi_{\infty}^{j}\in H^{1},

and, for
n\geq 1
and
J\geq 0
, a decomposition of the form

\xi_{n}(0)=\sum_{j=0}^{J}\lambda_{n}^{j}+\gamma_{n}^{J},\quad\text{where}\quad\lambda_{n}^{j}:=\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n}^{j};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{j}}\varphi_{\infty}^{j}

and the remainder term satisfies

\lim_{J\to J^{*}}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{4}_{x}}=0.

(6.1)

We denote

\sigma^{j}:=\lim_{n\to\infty}s^{j}_{n}\in\{0,\pm\infty\},\quad y^{j}:=\lim_{n\to\infty}|y^{j}_{n}|\in\{0,\infty\}

for
j\geqslant 0
. Then (by the orthogonality of parameters) we have
(\sigma^{0},y^{0})=(0,0)
and
(\sigma^{j},y^{j})\neq(0,0)
for
j\geqslant 1
. Recall that for each pair
(j,k)
, either
s_{n}^{j}\equiv s_{n}^{k}
or
\sign(s_{n}^{j}-s_{n}^{k})\in\{\pm 1\}
is independent of
n
.

If
J^{*}\geqslant 1
, we have
\varphi_{\infty}^{j}\neq 0
for
j\in[1,J^{*}]
. If
J^{*}
is finite, we use the convention that
\varphi_{\infty}^{j}=\gamma_{n}^{j}=0
for
j\geqslant J^{*}+1
and
n\geqslant 1
, and the parameters
(y_{n}^{j},\sigma_{n}^{j})

for
j\geqslant J^{*}+1
are chosen so that
y^{j}=\infty
for all
j\geqslant J^{*}+1
and
the orthogonality of
\{(y_{n}^{j},\sigma_{n}^{j})\}_{j\geqslant 1}
holds.

We now let
\varepsilon>0
to be determined below and choose
J^{\dagger}=J^{\dagger}(\varepsilon)\leqslant J^{*}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{J\geqslant J^{\dagger}}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{(L^{\infty}_{t}\cap L^{8}_{t})L^{4}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}\leqslant\varepsilon,

(6.2)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=J^{\dagger}+1}^{\infty}\left\lVert\varphi_{\infty}^{j}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}\leqslant\varepsilon^{2}.

(6.3)

Note that that smallness of the
L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}
-norm follows from (
6.1
) combined with interpolation, Strichartz, and the uniform
H^{1}
bound on
\gamma_{n}^{J}
. The parameter
\varepsilon>0
will be chosen in a way that depends only on
\sup_{n}\left\lVert\xi_{n}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{1}}
and
\sup_{n}\left\lVert z_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R})}
(and several universal constants); see Remark
6.13
below.

In the construction above, we have labeled the profiles
\varphi_{\infty}^{j}
using the index
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. In what follows, it will be convenient to have a different system for labeling profiles with
0\leq j\leq J^{\dagger}
. In particular, each
j
will correspond to a unique pair
(s,k)\in\mathbb{Z}\times[\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}]
.

Definition 6.1

(Relabeling via the mapping
j\mapsto(s,k)
)
.

By construction, for each distinct pair
j,k\in[0,\infty)
, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}s_{n}^{j}-s_{n}^{k}=\infty,\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}s_{n}^{k}-s_{n}^{j}=\infty,\quad\text{or}\quad s_{n}^{j}\equiv s_{n}^{k}.

This allows us to put the set of sequences ‘in order’. For example,
(s_{n}^{j})>(s_{n}^{k})
if
s_{n}^{j}-s_{n}^{k}\to\infty
. In particular, we can map the set of indices
j\in[0,J^{\dagger}]
to
s(j)\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]\cap\mathbb{Z}
in such a way that
(s_{n}^{j})>(s_{n}^{k})
if and only if
s(j)>s(k)
(and similarly
s_{n}^{j}\equiv s_{n}^{k}
if and only if
s(j)=s(k)
). We choose
s(0)=0
, so that
s_{\min}\leq 0\leq s_{\max}
and
s_{\max}-s_{\min}\leq J^{\dagger}
.

We define
s(j)
for
j>J^{\dagger}
as follows: First, if
s_{n}^{j}\equiv s_{n}^{k}
for some
k\in[0,J^{\dagger}]
, we set
s(j)=s(k)
. Otherwise, we set
s(j)=s_{\min}-1
.

Next, we define
k(j)
. First, if
s(j)\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]
and
y^{j}=0
, we set
k(j)=0
. If instead
y^{j}=\infty
, then we let
k(j)
equal the number of
m\in[0,j]
such that
s_{n}^{s}\equiv s_{n}^{j}
and
y^{m}=\infty
. Second, if
s(j)=s_{\min}-1
, then we let
k(j)
equal the number of
m\in[0,j)
such that
s_{n}^{j}\equiv s_{n}^{m}
.

It follows that
j\mapsto(s(j),k(j))
