is injective. Furthermore, we have the following monotonicity property: if
j_{1}<j_{2}
,
s(j_{1})=s(j_{2})\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]
, and
y^{j_{1}}=y^{j_{2}}=\infty
, then
1\leq k(j_{1})<k(j_{2})
.

With this new labeling system in place, we next introduce a modified remainder and a modified form of the profile decomposition. We first define the following:

Definition 6.2

(Modified Remainder)
.

Define

S=\{j\in(J^{\dagger},\infty):s(j)\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]\}.

For
J>J^{\dagger}
, let
S_{J}=\{j\in(J^{\dagger},J]:s(j)\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]\}
and

\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}:=\gamma_{n}^{J^{\dagger}}-\sum_{j\in S_{J}}\lambda_{n}^{j}.

In particular, while
\gamma_{n}^{J}
is defined by removing all profiles indexed by
j\in(J^{\dagger}+1,J]
from
\gamma_{n}^{J^{\dagger}}
, the modified remainder
\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}
is obtained by removing only those profiles whose time-translation sequences are equivalent to one that already appears in the first
J^{\dagger}+1
profiles. Equivalently, we can say that we obtain
\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}
from
\gamma_{n}^{J}
by adding back in those profiles that are not equivalent (with respect to the time-translation parameters) to any of the first
J^{\dagger}+1
profiles.

Next, let us define

J_{0}=\max\bigl\{J^{\dagger},\max\{j\geqslant 0\ |\ s(j)\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]\quad\text{and}\quad k(j)=0\}\bigr\}.

In particular,
J_{0}
is finite since
s_{\max}-s_{\min}\leq J^{\dagger}
and the map
j\mapsto(s(j),k(j))
is injective. Furthermore, if
J^{*}<\infty
then
J_{0}\leqslant J^{*}
; this is due to our convention that
y^{j}=\infty
for
j\geqslant J^{*}+1
.

Finally, for
s\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]
and
J\geqslant 1
, we let

K(s,J)=\#\{m\in[1,J]\ |\ s(m)=s,\,y^{m}=\infty\}.

(6.4)

Then, for
J\geqslant J_{0}
, we may obtain the following modified profile decomposition:

\xi_{n}(0)=\sum_{s=s_{\min}}^{s_{\max}}\left(\lambda_{n}^{(s,0)}+\sum_{k=1}^{K(s,J)}\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}\right)+\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J},

(6.5)

where we have relabeled
\lambda_{n}^{j}
as
\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}
(with
s=s(j)
and
k=k(j)
), and we use the convention that
\lambda_{n}^{(s,0)}=0
if there is no
j\geqslant 1
such that
(s(j),k(j))=(s,0)
.

Proof of (
6.5
).

By the profile decomposition at level
J^{\dagger}
and the definition of
\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}
, we have

\xi_{n}(0)=\sum_{j=0}^{J^{\dagger}}\lambda_{n}^{j}+\gamma_{n}^{J^{\dagger}}=\sum_{j=0}^{J^{\dagger}}\lambda_{n}^{j}+\sum_{j\in S_{J}}\lambda_{n}^{j}+\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}.

Then, by the definition of
S_{J}
and
K(s,J)
, we have

\{\lambda_{n}^{j}\}_{j=0}^{J^{\dagger}}\cup\{\lambda_{n}^{j}\}_{j\in S_{J}}=\{\lambda_{n}^{\ell}:s(\ell)\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]\quad\text{and}\quad k(\ell)\in[0,K(s(\ell),J)]\},

which yields the result.
∎

Our next main step will be to define nonlinear profiles associated to each linear profile. Before doing so, let us record the following estimate on linear profiles. We first introduce the notation

\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}(t):=\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}

and remark that the time dependence may not always explicitly be indicated (and so will need to be understood from context). Essentially, the following estimate captures the fact that the
\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}
(for fixed
s
) concentrate the bulk of their norm around time
s_{n}^{j}
, where
s=s(j)
. Here and below, we use the following notation: Given
s\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]
, we have
s=s(j)
for at least one
j\geq 0
. We denote the time sequence
s_{n}^{j}
by
s_{n}^{s}
, which is well-defined due to the fact that
s(j)=s(k)\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]
implies
s_{n}^{j}\equiv s_{n}^{k}
(cf. Definition
6.1
).

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
s,s^{\prime}\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]
. If
s\neq s^{\prime}
then

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\sup_{K\geqslant 0}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{k=0}^{K}\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}((-\infty,-\tau+s_{n}^{s^{\prime}}]\cup[\tau+s_{n}^{s^{\prime}},\infty))}=0.

Furthermore if two sequences
\{\tau_{n}^{1}\}
and
\{\tau_{n}^{2}\}
are such that
s_{n}^{s}-\tau_{n}^{1}\to\infty

and
\tau_{n}^{2}-s_{n}^{s}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
then

\sup_{K\geqslant 0}\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{k=0}^{K}\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}((-\infty,\tau_{n}^{1}]\cup[\tau_{n}^{2},\infty))}=0.

Proof.

We only treat the first estimate, as the other is handled similarly. Note that for any
\varepsilon>0
there exists
K_{0}
such that

\sup_{K\geqslant K_{0}}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{k=K_{0}}^{K}\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\left(\sum_{k=K_{0}}^{\infty}\left\lVert\varphi^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leqslant\varepsilon,

thanks to Strichartz estimate and orthogonality.
3
3

3

Here (and below) we write
\varphi^{(s,k)}
for the profile
\varphi^{j}_{\infty}
with
(s,k)=(s(j),s(k))
.
Thus, it suffices to show that

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(-\infty,-\tau+s_{n}^{s^{\prime}}]\cup[\tau+s_{n}^{s^{\prime}},\infty))}=0

for each
k\geqslant 0
. When
k=0
, one has
\lambda_{n}^{(s,0)}(t)=\mathcal{L}(t;s_{n}^{s};z_{n})\varphi^{(s,0)}
. Thus, using Proposition
4.8
and interpolation,

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot;0;z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s^{\prime}}))\varphi^{(s,0)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}=0.

On the other hand, when
|y_{n}^{(s,k)}|\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, the estimate follows from the fact that

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\left\lVert e^{-it\Delta}\varphi^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}(-\infty,-\tau]\cup[\tau,\infty))}=0

in view of (
5.17
) and Lemma
5.2
.
∎

6.1.
Definition of nonlinear profiles

The next step is to define corresponding
nonlinear profiles
for each
j
, as well as the
nonlinear remainder
for each
J
. The construction depends on the behavior of the parameters
(s_{n}^{j},y_{n}^{j})
.

Definition 6.4

(Nonlinear profiles for
y^{j}=0
)
.

Suppose that

(s(j),k(j))=(s,0)\quad\text{for some}\quad s\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}].

Passing to a subsequence, we have

(z_{n}(t+s_{n}^{j}),\xi_{n}(t+s_{n}^{j}))\rightharpoonup(z_{\infty}^{j}(t),\xi_{\infty}^{j}(t))

weakly in
\mathbb{C}\times H^{1}
, with uniform convergence on any compact time interval. We then define

\Lambda_{n}^{(s,0)}(t):=\xi_{\infty}^{j}(t-s_{n}^{j}),

which solves (
3.8
) with
z_{\infty}^{j}(t-s_{n}^{j})
. We then define

u_{\infty}^{s}(t):=\Phi[z_{\infty}^{j}(t)]+R[z_{\infty}^{j}(t)]\xi_{\infty}^{j}(t),

which is a solution to (
1.1
).

Definition 6.5

(Nonlinear profiles for
y^{j}=\infty
and
\sigma^{j}=0
)
.

Suppose that

(s(j),k(j))=(0,k)\quad\text{for some}\quad k\geqslant 1.

Then we define
\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}
to be the solution to

\begin{cases}&(i{\partial}_{t}+H)\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}=B[z_{n}]\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}+\tilde{N}(z_{n},\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}),\\
&\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}(0)=P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{j}}\varphi_{\infty}^{j},\end{cases}

where
\tilde{N}
is as in (
3.7
).

The nonlinear profiles for the remaining case will be defined in terms of a parameter
\tau>0
, although we will not express this dependence explicitly. Later, we will need to take a limit as
\tau\to\infty
.

Definition 6.6

(Nonlinear profiles for
y^{j}=\infty
and
\sigma^{j}=\infty
)
.

Suppose that

(s(j),k(j))=(s,k)\quad\text{for some}\quad s\in[1,s_{\max}]\quad\text{and}\quad k\geqslant 1.

Let
v_{n}^{j}
be the solution to

\begin{cases}&(i{\partial}_{t}+H)v_{n}^{j}=B[z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{j})]v_{n}^{j}+\tilde{N}(z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{j}),v_{n}^{j}),\\
&v_{n}^{j}(-\tau)=P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{j}}e^{i\tau\Delta}\varphi_{\infty}^{j},\end{cases}

where
\tilde{N}
is as in (
3.7
). Then we define
\Lambda^{(s,k)}_{n}
by

\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}(t):=v_{n}^{j}(t-s_{n}^{j}).

Nonlinear profiles corresponding to
(s,k)
with
s<0
and
k\geqslant 1
are defined similarly; however, we can omit their definition here, as we will only consider the positive time direction in what follows.

Definition 6.7

(Nonlinear Remainder)
.

We define the
nonlinear remainder

\Gamma_{n}^{J}\in C(I_{n};P_{c}H^{1})

to be the solution to

\begin{cases}&(i{\partial}_{t}+H)\Gamma_{n}^{J}=B[z_{n}]\Gamma_{n}^{J}+\tilde{N}(z_{n},\Gamma^{J}_{n}),\\
&\Gamma_{n}^{J}(0)=\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J},\end{cases}

where
\tilde{N}
is as in (
3.7
).

The main ingredient to complete the proof of Proposition
3.7
will be to establish the following proposition by a contradiction argument (recall the definition of
(M_{*},E_{*})
in (
3.9
)):

Proposition 6.8
.

There exist
s_{+}\in[0,s_{\max}]
and
s_{-}\in[s_{\min},0]
such that

(\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s_{\pm}}),\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{\infty}^{s_{\pm}}))=(M_{*},E_{*}).

(6.6)

We note that Proposition
6.14
below guarantees that

\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s})\leqslant M_{*}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{\infty}^{s})\leqslant E_{*}\quad\text{for}\quad s\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}].

Thus, by (
3.11
), the failure of (
6.6
) guarantees scattering for
u_{\infty}^{s}
.

6.2.
Estimates on the nonlinear remainder

We begin by estimating the nonlinear remainder term. We first need some estimates for the modified linear remainder
\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}
. Recall that
\varepsilon,J^{\dagger}
were introduced above, but have not yet been specified.

Lemma 6.9
.

We have the following estimates on
\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{J>J^{\dagger}}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}\leqslant\sup_{n}\left\lVert\xi_{n}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}+C_{1}^{2}\varepsilon^{2},

(6.7)

\displaystyle\sup_{J>J^{\dagger}}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{(L^{8}_{t}\cap L^{\infty}_{t})L^{4}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}\leqslant(1+C_{1}(C_{2}+C_{3}))\varepsilon,

(6.8)

where
C_{1},C_{2}
are constants arising from the uniform Strichartz estimate and
C_{3}
is the implicit constant the
H^{1}\hookrightarrow L^{4}
Sobolev embedding.

Proof.

By the orthogonality of the linear profiles, for all
J\geqslant J^{\dagger}
we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}-\gamma_{n}^{J^{\dagger}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in S_{J}}\left\lVert\lambda_{n}^{j}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}+o_{n}(1)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{1}^{2}\sum_{j>J^{\dagger}}\left\lVert\varphi_{\infty}^{j}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}+o_{n}(1)\leqslant(C_{1}\varepsilon)^{2}+o_{n}(1)\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

Thus

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}\leqslant\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{J^{\dagger}}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}+C_{2}C_{1}\varepsilon+o_{n}(1),

which implies

\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}\leqslant(1+C_{1}C_{2})\varepsilon\quad\text{for}\quad J>J^{\dagger}.

Similarly, by Sobolev embedding,

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}\leqslant\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{J^{\dagger}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}+C_{3}C_{1}\varepsilon+o_{n}(1)

by Sobolev embedding. Thus (
6.8
) follows from (
6.2
).

Finally,

\left\lVert\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}\leqslant\left\lVert\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J^{\dagger}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}+C_{1}^{2}\sum_{j>J^{\dagger}}\left\lVert\varphi^{j}_{\infty}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}+o_{n}(1),

which yields

\sup_{J>J^{\dagger}}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}\leqslant\sup_{n}\left\lVert\xi_{n}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}+C_{1}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}.

∎

Corollary 6.10
.

There exists
J^{\dagger}
sufficiently large such that for any
J>J^{\dagger}
, there exists
N(J)
such that if
n\geqslant N(J)
then the nonlinear remainder
\Gamma_{n}^{J}
is well-defined on
\mathbb{R}
and obeys the following bounds

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\sup_{n}\|z_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}}+\sup_{n}\left\lVert\xi_{n}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}-\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}+\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{[z_{n};0,\infty;\infty]}\ll\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(I_{n})},

where the implicit constants are independent of
n
and
J
and the semi-norm refers to the notation introduced in (
4.7
). Furthermore,

\sup_{J>J^{\dagger}}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\varepsilon.

(6.9)

Proof.

The existence and first set of estimates follow from Lemma
4.11
, so we turn to (
6.9
). As in the proof of Lemma
4.11
, the Sobolev embedding
H^{1}\hookrightarrow L^{4}
and the
uniform Strichartz estimate give us

\displaystyle\|\Gamma^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}L^{4}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}(\left\lVert z_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}}).

Thus, using (
6.8
) and the bound on
\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1}}
, we find

\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\varepsilon+\varepsilon(z_{*}+\sup_{n}\left\lVert\xi_{n}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{1}})(\left\lVert z_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\varepsilon)\lesssim\varepsilon.

for all
J>J^{\dagger}
and
n\geq N(J)
.∎

We next consider the linear evolution of
\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}
. We recall the notation
s_{n}^{s}
introduced above (i.e.
s_{n}^{s}
denotes the common sequence
s_{n}^{j}
for all
j
such that
s(j)=s
).

Proposition 6.11
.

For any
s\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]
,

\lim_{J\to\infty}\sup_{\tau>0}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{4}_{x}([-\tau+s_{n}^{s},\tau+s_{n}^{s}])}=0.

(6.10)

Proof.

If instead we have

\varlimsup_{J\to\infty}\sup_{\tau>0}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{4}_{x}([-\tau+s_{n}^{s},\tau+s_{n}^{s}])}\geqslant c_{0}

for some
s\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]
and
c_{0}>0
, then we may find a subsequence of
J\to\infty
and
\tau=\tau(J)>0
such that

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{4}_{x}([-\tau+s_{n}^{s},\tau+s_{n}^{s}])}\geqslant\tfrac{1}{2}{c_{0}}

along a subsequence of
n
(depending on
J
).

Now, given
N\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have

\left\lVert P_{\geqslant N}\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{4}}\lesssim\left\lVert P_{\geqslant N}\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{H^{\frac{3}{4}}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

Thus, for
N
is sufficiently large, we see that

\left\lVert P_{<N}\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{4}_{x}([-\tau+s_{n}^{s},\tau+s_{n}^{s}])}\geqslant\tfrac{1}{4}{c_{0}}.

On the other hand, by Hölder and Strichartz, we have

\displaystyle\|P_{<N}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{4}_{x}([-\tau+s_{n}^{s},\tau+s_{n}^{s}])}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert P_{<N}\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}([-\tau+s_{n}^{s},\tau+s_{n}^{s}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert P_{<N}\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}([-\tau+s_{n}^{s},\tau+s_{n}^{s}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{2}},

and hence

\left\lVert P_{<N}\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}([-\tau+s_{n}^{s},\tau+s_{n}^{s}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\gtrsim_{c_{0}}1.

In particular, for each
J
in the subsequence, there exist
x_{n}^{J}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and

\tilde{s}_{n}^{J}\in[-\tau+s_{n}^{s},\tau+s_{n}^{s}]

such that
T_{x_{n}^{J}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}_{n}^{J},0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\rightharpoonup\tilde{\psi}^{J}\in H^{1}
weakly in
H^{1}
, where

\inf_{J}\|\tilde{\psi}^{J}\|_{H^{1}}\gtrsim 1.

We now write

T_{x_{n}^{J}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{s},0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}=[T_{x_{n}^{J}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{s},\tilde{s}_{n}^{J};z_{n})T_{x_{n}^{J}}][T_{x_{n}^{J}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}_{n}^{J},0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}]

and observe that since
\sup_{n}|s_{n}^{s}-\tilde{s}_{n}^{J}|\leq\tau<\infty
, the first operator above converges strongly to an operator on
H^{1}
with norm
\sim 1
(with precise value depending on
\sup_{n}\|z_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}
). Thus, we deduce that there exists
\psi^{J}\in H^{1}
such that

T_{x_{n}^{J}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{s},0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\rightharpoonup\psi^{J},\quad\text{with}\quad\inf_{J}\|\psi^{J}\|_{H^{1}}\gtrsim 1.

By the usual adjustments, we may also assume that
x_{n}^{J}\equiv 0
or
|x_{n}^{J}|\to\infty
.

We now claim that for each
J
in the subsequence, there exists
c\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and
k_{0}\in[J+1,\infty)
so that

T_{c}\psi^{J}=\varphi_{\infty}^{k_{0}},\quad\text{with}\quad s(k_{0})\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}],

where
\varphi_{\infty}^{k_{0}}
is the function given in the profile decomposition of
\{\xi_{n}(0)\}
. With this claim, we thereby obtain the contradiction

\lVert\psi^{J}\rVert_{H^{1}}=\lVert\varphi_{\infty}^{k_{0}}\rVert_{H^{1}}\leqslant\biggl(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}\left\lVert\varphi_{\infty}^{j}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}\biggr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad J\to\infty.

By construction, we may write

\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{n}^{J^{\dagger}}-\sum_{j\in S_{J}}\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n}^{j};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{j}}\varphi_{\infty}^{j}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{n}^{J}+\sum_{j\in(J^{\dagger},J]\backslash S_{J}}\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n}^{j};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{j}}\varphi_{\infty}^{j}.

Now, for any
j\in(J^{\dagger},J]\backslash S_{J}
, we have
|s_{n}^{s}-s_{n}^{j}|\to\infty
, and hence by Lemma
5.5
(ii),

T_{x_{n}^{J}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{s},0;z_{n})\mathcal{L}(0,s_{n}^{j};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{j}}\varphi^{j}_{\infty}\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty,

which implies

T_{x_{n}^{J}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{s},0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{J}\rightharpoonup\psi^{J}.

Next, we recall that by construction, we have

\lim_{K\to\infty}\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\gamma_{n}^{K}\})=0,

and thus we may find
K_{0}>J
so that
\eta(\{z_{n}\},\{\gamma_{n}^{K_{0}}\})<\tfrac{1}{10}\|\psi^{J}\|_{H^{1}}
. Then, writing

T_{x_{n}^{J}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{s},0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{K_{0}}=T_{x_{n}^{J}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{s},0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{J}-\sum_{j=J+1}^{K_{0}}T_{x_{n}^{J}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{s},s_{n}^{j};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{j}}\varphi_{\infty}^{j},

it follows that there exists
k_{0}\in[J+1,K_{0}]
and
g\in H^{1}\backslash\{0\}
such that

T_{x_{n}^{J}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{s},s_{n}^{k_{0}};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}^{k_{0}}}\varphi_{\infty}^{k_{0}}\rightharpoonup g.

By Lemma
5.5
(i)–(ii) and construction, this implies that
s_{n}^{s}\equiv s_{n}^{k_{0}}
and
\sup_{n}|x_{n}^{J}-y_{n}^{j_{0}}|<\infty
. In particular, we deduce that
s(k_{0})\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]
, and that

T_{y_{n}^{k_{0}}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{k_{0}},0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{J}\rightharpoonup T_{c}\psi^{J}\quad\text{for some}\quad c\in\mathbb{R}^{3}.

However, again by construction, we have that

T_{y_{n}^{k_{0}}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(s_{n}^{k_{0}},0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{J}\rightharpoonup\varphi_{\infty}^{k_{0}},

and thus
T_{c}\psi^{J}=\varphi_{\infty}^{k_{0}}
, as desired.
∎

We now estimate the nonlinear remainder. This estimate and its proof are closely related to
[
19
, Equation (7.18)]
and the proof therein.

Proposition 6.12
.

If
\mathcal{N}_{0}=\sup_{n}\left\lVert z_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\mu_{0}
is sufficiently small and
J^{\dagger}
is sufficiently large, then for any
s\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]
,

\sup_{\tau>0}\lim_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}([-\tau+s_{n}^{s},\tau+s_{n}^{s}])}=0.

(6.11)

Furthermore, for any
s\in[1,s_{\max}]
,

\sup_{\tau>0}\lim_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,-\tau+s_{n}^{s};z_{n})\Gamma_{n}^{J}(-\tau+s_{n}^{s})\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}([-\tau+s_{n}^{s},\tau+s_{n}^{s}])}=0.

(6.12)

Proof.

Following
[
19
, Proof of (7.18)]
, we introduce

\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{X^{j}_{n}}:=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left\langle t-s^{j}_{n}\right\rangle^{-\delta}\left\lVert f(t)\right\rVert_{(L^{4}+L^{p})_{x}},

for some
p>8
and
\delta\in(\tfrac{1}{8},\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{3}{p})
(e.g.
(p,\delta)=(10,\tfrac{1}{6})
).

Recalling (
6.8
), we have that for any
\tau>0
and
J\geqslant J^{\dagger}+1
,

\displaystyle\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{X^{j}_{n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{4}_{x}([-\tau+s_{n}^{j},\tau+s_{n}^{j}])}

\displaystyle\quad+\tau^{-\delta}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}L^{4}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\tau^{-\delta}+\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{4}_{x}([-\tau+s_{n}^{j},\tau+s_{n}^{j}])}.

Thus, letting first
J\to\infty
and then
\tau\to\infty
, we see from (
6.10
) that

\lim_{J\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{X^{j}_{n}}=0.

(6.13)

It suffices consider the case
\sigma^{j}=\infty
(the other case is similar). Using the dispersive estimate for
\mathcal{L}
(see Lemma
4.10
), we obtain

\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}(t)-\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})\gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{4}+L^{p}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}h(t-t^{\prime})\left\lVert\tilde{N}(z_{n},\Gamma^{J}_{n})\right\rVert_{L^{4/3}\cap L^{p^{\prime}}}dt^{\prime},

where
h(t)=\min\{|t|^{-(\frac{3}{2}-\frac{3}{p})},|t|^{-\frac{3}{4}}\}.
We now observe that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\tilde{N}(z_{n},\Gamma^{J}_{n})\right\rVert_{L^{4/3}}\lesssim\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{4}+L^{p}}\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{2p}{p-2}}}(|z_{n}|+\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{4}}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\tilde{N}(z_{n},\Gamma^{J}_{n})\right\rVert_{L^{p^{\prime}}}\lesssim\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{4}+L^{p}}\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{2p}{p-2}}}(|z_{n}|+\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\frac{2p}{p-2}}}),

which (together with (
6.9
)) imply

\displaystyle\|\tilde{N}(z_{n},\Gamma^{J}_{n})\|_{L^{4/3}\cap L^{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigl[|z_{n}|^{2}+\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{2}}^{2}\bigr]\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{4}+L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigl[\mathcal{N}_{0}^{2}+\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}L^{4}}^{2}\bigr]\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{4}+L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigl[\mathcal{N}_{0}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\bigr]\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{4}+L^{p}}.

We therefore obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}(t)-\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{4}+L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\mathcal{N}_{0}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{X_{n}^{j}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\langle t^{\prime}-s_{n}^{j}\right\rangle^{\delta}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\mathcal{N}_{0}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\left\lVert\Gamma^{J}_{n}\right\rVert_{X_{n}^{j}}\left\langle t-s_{n}^{j}\right\rangle^{\delta},

where we have used
-3(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p})+\delta<-1
.
Thus, if
\mathcal{N}_{0}+\varepsilon
is sufficiently small,

\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}-\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{X_{n}^{j}}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{X_{n}^{j}}.

(6.14)

In particular, we see from (
6.13
) that

\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{X_{n}^{j}}\leqslant 2\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{X_{n}^{j}}\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad J\to\infty.

(6.15)

Now, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{4}}\leqslant\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{4}+L^{p}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{2p}{p-2}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Thus, for any
\tau>1
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([s_{n}^{j}-\tau,s_{n}^{j}+\tau])}

\displaystyle{}\leqslant\tau^{\delta/2}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{X_{n}^{j}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{4}(L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{2p}{p-2}})([s_{n}^{j}-\tau,s_{n}^{j}+\tau])}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim\tau^{\delta/2}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{X_{n}^{j}}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{4}H^{\frac{1}{6}}_{3}}+\tau^{\frac{4p}{p-6}}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{\frac{2p}{3}}L^{\frac{2p}{p-2}}})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim_{\tau}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{X_{n}^{j}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Using (
6.15
) and boundedness in
\mathrm{Stz}^{1}
, we obtain the desired estimate (
6.11
).

Finally, since
\Gamma_{n}^{J}
is a solution to the nonlinear equation, we see from Proposition
4.11
that (
6.11
) implies (
6.12
). ∎

With the estimates above in place, we can finally discuss our choice of
\varepsilon
(and hence
J^{\dagger}
) more precisely.

Remark 6.13

(Choice of
J^{\dagger}
)
.

We choose
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small so that (
6.14
) is valid. The implicit constants that are used to obtain this estimate depend on the choice of
p,\delta
(which we may fix to be
(p,\delta)=(10,\tfrac{1}{6})
, say), the constants in uniform Strichartz estimate, and that in (
6.9
). The implicit constant in (
6.9
) depends on the constants in uniform Strichartz estimate,
\sup_{n}\left\lVert z_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
, and
\sup_{n}\left\lVert\xi_{n}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{1}}
. The constants in the uniform Strichartz estimate in turn depend on
\sup_{n}\left\lVert z_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
. Thus, we find that the choice of
\varepsilon
depends only on
\sup_{n}\left\lVert\xi_{n}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{1}}
and
\sup_{n}\left\lVert z_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
.

6.3.
Estimates on nonlinear profiles

We will next derive estimates on the nonlinear profiles.

6.3.1.
Estimate on
\Lambda_{n}^{(s,0)}

Let us begin with the profile
\Lambda_{n}^{(s,0)}
, defined as in Definition
6.4
. Recalling the notation there, we first note that by
H^{1}
-subcriticality of (
1.1
) and uniform
H^{1}
-boundedness, we have that
(z_{\infty}^{s},\xi_{\infty}^{s})
is global in time.

Proposition 6.14
.

Suppose that (
3.16
) holds. Let
s\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]
and let
(z_{\infty}^{s},\xi_{\infty}^{s})
and
u_{\infty}^{s}
be as in Definition
6.4
. Then, for all
s\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]
, one has

\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s})\leqslant M_{*},\quad\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{\infty}^{s})\leqslant E_{*},

(6.16)

and
\left\lVert u_{\infty}^{s}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{\infty}^{s})+\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s}).

If
u^{s}_{\infty}
scatters to
\mathscr{S}_{0}
forward in time, then

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\bigl\{\left\lVert\xi_{\infty}^{s}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}+\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau;z_{\infty}^{s})\xi_{\infty}^{s}(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}\bigr\}=0.

The analogous statement holds backward in time.

Finally, if

(\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s}),\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{\infty}^{s}))\neq(M_{*},E_{*})

then
u_{\infty}^{s}(t)
scatters to
\mathscr{S}_{0}
in both time directions and
\left\lVert\xi_{\infty}^{s}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty
.

Proof.

We begin with (
6.16
). We apply a profile decomposition with
L^{4}
error to the bounded sequence
\{u_{n}(s_{n}^{s})\}_{n}\subset H^{1}
to get a decomposition of the form

u_{n}(s_{n}^{s})=u_{\infty}^{s}+\sum_{k=1}^{J}T_{y_{n}}\varphi^{k}+r_{n}^{J}.

We have

\displaystyle M_{*}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{M}(u_{n}(s_{n}^{s}))\geqslant\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s})+\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{k}),

\displaystyle E_{*}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{n})\geqslant\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{\infty}^{s})+\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{k}),

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{I}_{V}(u_{n})=\mathbb{I}_{V}(u_{\infty}^{s})+\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\mathbb{I}_{0}(\varphi^{k}).

Here the second and the third decomposition follows from the decomposition of
\mathbb{G}(u_{n})
. The first implies
\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s})\leqslant M_{*}
, yielding the former part of (
6.16
).

We next show that
\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{1})\geqslant 0
. It suffices to consider the case
\varphi^{1}\neq 0
. By assumption,
\mathbb{K}_{V,2}(u_{n})\geqslant\kappa>0
, which (together with (
3.10
) and the identity
\mathbb{I}_{0}(Q)=\mathbb{E}_{0}(Q)
) yields

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{I}_{V}(u_{n})=\lim_{n\to\infty}\bigl[\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{n})-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{K}_{V,2}(u_{n})\bigr]\leqslant E_{*}-\kappa<\tfrac{1}{M_{*}}\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{I}_{0}(Q)-\kappa.

Furthermore,

\mathbb{I}_{V}(u_{\infty}^{s})\geqslant-\tfrac{1}{2}\left\lVert(x\cdot\nabla V+2V)_{-}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s}),

so that

\mathbb{I}_{0}(\varphi^{1})<\tfrac{1}{M_{*}}\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{I}_{0}(Q)-\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2}\left\lVert(x\cdot\nabla V+2V)_{-}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s}).

As
\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{1})\leqslant M_{*}-\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{1})\mathbb{I}_{0}(\varphi^{1})<{}

\displaystyle\tfrac{M_{*}-\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s})}{M_{*}}\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{I}_{0}(Q)

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}\left\lVert(x\cdot\nabla V+2V)_{-}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}(M_{*}-\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s}))\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s}).

Now observe that if
M_{*}\lesssim_{Q,V}1
, then the right hand side is decreasing in
\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s})\in[0,M_{*}]
. Hence we obtain the bound

\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{1})\mathbb{I}_{0}(\varphi^{1})<\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{I}_{0}(Q).

As

\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{I}_{0}(Q)=\inf\{\mathbb{M}(\varphi)\mathbb{I}_{0}(\varphi)\ |\ \mathbb{K}_{0,2}(\varphi)\leqslant 0,\,\varphi\neq 0\},

we see that

\mathbb{K}_{0,2}(\varphi^{1})>0.

(6.17)

Thus

\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{1})=\mathbb{H}_{0}(\varphi^{1})-\mathbb{G}(\varphi^{1})=\tfrac{1}{3}\mathbb{H}_{0}(\varphi^{1})+\tfrac{1}{3}\mathbb{K}_{0,2}(\varphi^{1})>0,

(6.18)

and so we obtain
\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{1})\geqslant 0
also in the case
\varphi^{1}\neq 0
.

By the same argument, we see that
\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{k})\geqslant 0
for all
k\geqslant 1
, which yields
{\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{\infty}^{s})}\leqslant E_{*}
(i.e. the latter part of (
6.16
)). By the variational characterization of
\mathscr{E}_{1}(\mu)
, we see that
\mathbb{K}_{V,2}(u_{\infty}^{s})>0
, and hence
u_{\infty}^{s}
is a global solution obeying the bound
\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{\infty}^{s})+\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s}).
Scattering of
u_{\infty}^{s}
in the case
(\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s}),\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{\infty}^{s}))\neq(M_{*},E_{*})
follows from (
3.11
).
∎

Remark 6.15
.

In the above proof,
the upper bound for
\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{1})
is obtained similarly. Indeed, using the facts that
\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{\infty})\geqslant\mathscr{E}_{0}(\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}))
and
\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{k})\geqslant 0
for all
k\geqslant 2
, one has

\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{1})\leqslant E_{*}-\mathscr{E}_{0}(\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty})).

Hence, we obtain the bound

\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{1})\leqslant E_{*}-\mathscr{E}_{0}(M_{*}).

(6.19)

By using (
3.10
), we may also obtain

\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{1})\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{1})<\tfrac{M_{*}-\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty})}{M_{*}}\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{E}_{0}(Q)-\mathcal{E}_{0}(\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}))(M_{*}-\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}))-\delta_{0}\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{1}).

As
\mathcal{E}_{0}(\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}))\sim e_{0}\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty})
, one sees that the right hand side is decreasing in
\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty})\in[0,M_{*}]
if
M_{*}\lesssim_{Q,e_{0}}1
. Thus we obtain

\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{1})<\frac{1}{\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{1})}\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{E}_{0}(Q)-\delta_{0}.

(6.20)

We will use these estimates in the estimate of
\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}
.

6.3.2.
Estimate on
\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}

To estimate the
\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}
, the idea is to compare the solution to the translation of the solution to the cubic NLS without potential:

\begin{cases}i{\partial}_{t}w_{\infty}^{(0,k)}-\Delta w_{\infty}^{(0,k)}=\sigma|w_{\infty}^{(0,k)}|^{2}w_{\infty}^{(0,k)},\\
w_{\infty}^{(0,k)}(0)=\varphi^{(0,k)},\end{cases}

(6.21)

where we have written
\varphi^{(0,k)}
to denote the profile
\varphi_{\infty}^{j}
appearing in Definition
6.5
. We first collect some relevant properties of
w_{\infty}^{(0,k)}
.

Lemma 6.16
.

Suppose (
3.16
) holds. Let
k\geqslant 1
and let

\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}(t)
be as in Definition
6.5
.
If
\varphi^{(0,k)}\neq 0
then
\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{(0,k)})\leqslant M_{*}
,

0<\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{(0,k)})\leqslant\min\bigl[E_{*}-\mathscr{E}_{0}(M_{*}),\tfrac{1}{\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{(0,k)})}\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{E}_{0}(Q)-\delta_{0}\bigr],

(6.22)

and

\mathbb{K}_{0,2}(\varphi^{(0,k)})>0.

In particular, there exists a unique global solution
w_{\infty}^{(0,k)}(t)\in C(\mathbb{R},H^{1})
to (
6.21
)
such that

\left\lVert w_{\infty}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim_{\delta_{0}}1,\quad\text{and}\quad\left\lVert w_{\infty}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{(0,k)})+\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{(0,k)}).

Proof.

We apply the
H^{1}\hookrightarrow L^{4}
profile decomposition to obtain

u_{n}(0)=u_{\infty}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}T_{\hat{y}_{n}^{\ell}}\hat{\varphi}^{\ell}+\hat{r}_{n}^{L}.

As

\displaystyle(T_{y_{n}^{(0,k)}})^{-1}u_{n}={}

\displaystyle(T_{y_{n}^{(0,k)}})^{-1}\Phi[z_{n}]+(T_{y_{n}^{(0,k)}})^{-1}\xi_{n}+(T_{y_{n}^{(0,k)}})^{-1}(R[z_{n}]-1)\xi_{n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup{}

\displaystyle 0+\varphi^{(0,k)}+0=\varphi^{(0,k)}\neq 0

weakly in
H^{1}
as
n\to\infty
, it follows that
T_{y_{n}^{(0,k)}}\varphi^{(0,k)}
appears in the above decomposition. That is, there exists
\ell_{0}
and
y_{\delta}:=\lim_{n\to\infty}[\hat{y}_{n}^{\ell_{0}}-y_{n}^{(0,k)}]\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that
\varphi^{(0,k)}=T_{y_{\delta}}\hat{\varphi}^{\ell_{0}}.
Thus, arguing as in the proof of (
6.17
), we obtain
\mathbb{K}_{0}(\varphi^{(0,k)})>0
. Similarly, we obtain the energy bound from (
6.18
), (
6.19
), and (
6.20
).

The global existence of
w_{\infty}^{(0,k)}
therefore follows from the well-posedness theory for the standard cubic NLS (see e.g.
[
6
]
). ∎

For the
\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}
, we will prove the following:

Proposition 6.17
.

Suppose that (
3.16
) holds and fix
k\geq 1
. Let
\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}
be as in Definition
6.5
, and let
w_{\infty}^{(0,k)}\in C(\mathbb{R},H^{1})
be the unique global solution to (
6.21
) given in Lemma
6.16
. Writing
y_{n}
for the sequence
y_{n}^{(0,k)}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}-T_{y_{n}}w_{\infty}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle=0,

(6.23)

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert{\bf 1}_{\{|x-y_{n}|\geqslant R\}}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle=0,

(6.24)

and

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\biggl\{\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,-\tau;z_{n})\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}(-\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}((-\infty,-\tau))}\\
+\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau;z_{n})\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}((\tau,\infty))}+\left\lVert\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}((-\infty,-\tau)\cup(\tau,\infty))}\biggr\}=0.

(6.25)

Furthermore, for any
\tau>0
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}([-\tau,\tau])}=\left\lVert w_{\infty}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}([-\tau,\tau])}.

(6.26)

We begin with the following lemma.

Lemma 6.18
.

Let
\varphi\in H^{1}
and let
\{y_{n}\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a sequence such that
|y_{n}|\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, and let
\{z_{n}\}\subset\mathrm{SBC}
. Let
v_{n}
be the solution to the first equation in (
3.8
) with
v_{n}(0)=P_{c}T_{y_{n}}\varphi
.

Let
A>0
and
\frac{1}{2}<\theta_{1}<\theta_{2}\leqslant 1
.
If

\sup_{n}\left\lVert v_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{2}}([-\tau,\tau]))}\leqslant A

(6.27)

for some
\tau>0
, then
v_{n}
satisfies the following:

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert v_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}([-\tau,\tau]\times\{|x-y_{n}|\geqslant R\})}

\displaystyle=0,

(6.28)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}(i{\partial}_{t}v_{n}-\Delta v_{n}-\sigma|v_{n}|^{2}v_{n})\,ds\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{1}}([-\tau,\tau])}

\displaystyle=0.

(6.29)

Proof of Lemma
6.18
.

As in the proof of Lemma
5.2
, we let
\chi_{n,r}(x)=\chi(\tfrac{x-y_{n}}{r})
be a smooth function obeying

\chi_{n,r}(x)=\begin{cases}0&|x-y_{n}|<r\\
1&|x-y_{n}|>2r\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\nabla\chi_{n,r}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\tfrac{1}{r}.

We first note that

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}|v_{n}|^{2}=2\nabla\cdot\operatorname{Im}(\overline{v_{n}}\nabla v_{n})+2\operatorname{Im}(\overline{v_{n}}B[z_{n}]v_{n})+2\operatorname{Im}(\overline{v_{n}}\tilde{N}(z_{n},v_{n})),

which yields

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}|^{2}dx={}

\displaystyle-2\int\nabla\chi_{n,r}\cdot\operatorname{Im}(\overline{v_{n}}\nabla v_{n})\,dx+2\int\chi_{n,r}\operatorname{Im}(\overline{v_{n}}B[z_{n}]v_{n})\,dx

(6.30)

\displaystyle+2\int\chi_{n,r}\operatorname{Im}(\overline{v_{n}}\tilde{N}(z_{n},v_{n}))\,dx.

For the first term in (
6.30
), we observe that

\left\lvert\int\nabla\chi_{n,r}\cdot\operatorname{Im}(\overline{v_{n}}\nabla v_{n})dx\right\rvert\lesssim\tfrac{1}{r}\left\lVert v_{n}\right\rVert_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2},

which we prove by interpolation: It suffices to prove that
|\nabla|^{s}[\nabla\chi_{n,r}]|\nabla|^{-s}
maps
L^{2}\to L^{2}
with norm
\lesssim r^{-1}
for
s=\tfrac{1}{2}
, which in turn follows provided we verify this bound for
s\in\{0,1\}
. The case
s=0
is straightforward, while for
s=1
we use the product rule, Sobolev embedding, and the fact that
\|\Delta\chi\|_{L^{3}}\lesssim r^{-1}
.

The second term in (
6.30
) is estimated as in the proof of Lemma
5.2
. In particular, one has

\left\lVert B[z_{n}(t)]v_{n}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left(\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+o_{n}(1)

as
n\to\infty
, and hence

\left\lvert\int\chi_{n,r}\operatorname{Im}(\overline{v_{n}}B[z_{n}]v_{n})dx\right\rvert\leqslant\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}|^{2}dx+o_{n}(1).

We turn to the third term in (
6.30
). We observe the following bounds:

\displaystyle|\chi_{n,r}^{\frac{1}{2}}P_{c}N(z_{n},R[z_{n}]v_{n})|\lesssim|\chi_{n,r}^{\frac{1}{2}}R[z_{n}]v_{n}|(|\Phi[z]|^{2}+|R[z_{n}]v_{n}|^{2}),

\displaystyle|\chi_{n,r}^{\frac{1}{2}}R[z_{n}]v_{n}|\leqslant|\chi_{n,r}^{\frac{1}{2}}v_{n}|+|\phi_{0}|\biggl\{\biggl[\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}|^{2}dx\biggr]^{\frac{1}{2}}+o_{n}(1)\biggr\}.

Thus

\left\lVert\chi_{n,r}^{\frac{1}{2}}P_{c}N(z_{n},R[z_{n}]v_{n})\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leqslant C\left(\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+o_{n}(1).

Inserting these estimates into (
6.30
), we see that

f(t)=\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}(t)|^{2}\,dx\quad\text{satisfies}\quad f(t)\leqslant f(0)+\tfrac{\tau}{r}[C+o_{n}(1)]+C\int_{0}^{t}f(s)\,ds

for some constant
C>0
(independent of
t,r,n
). Thus, by Gronwall’s inequality,

\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}f(t)\leqslant e^{C\tau}\left(\int\chi_{n,r}|\varphi|^{2}dx+\tfrac{\tau}{r}(C+o_{n}(1)\right).

In particular, given
\varepsilon,\tau>0
, we may find
r>0
sufficiently large and
N
so that

n\geq N\implies\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\int\chi_{n,r}|v_{n}(t)|^{2}dx\leqslant\varepsilon.

As we can derive a similar estimate for negative times, we obtain (
6.28
).

It remains to prove (
6.29
). Defining

w_{n}(t):=T_{y_{n}}^{-1}v_{n}(t),

we may write

\displaystyle-iT_{y_{n}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}e^{-i(t-s)\Delta}(i{\partial}_{t}v_{n}-\Delta v_{n}-\sigma|v_{n}|^{2}v_{n})\,ds

\displaystyle=w_{n}(t)-e^{-it\Delta}\varphi+i\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}(\sigma|w_{n}|^{2}w_{n})\,ds=N_{1}+N_{2}+N_{3},

where

\displaystyle N_{1}=(T_{y_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}-e^{-it\Delta})\varphi,

\displaystyle N_{2}=-i\int_{0}^{t}(T_{y_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(t,s;z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}-e^{-i(t-s)\Delta})(\sigma|w_{n}|^{2}w_{n})(s)\,ds,

\displaystyle N_{3}=-i\int_{0}^{t}(T_{y_{n}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}(t,s;z_{n})(\tilde{N}(z_{n},T_{y_{n}}w_{n})-\sigma P_{c}T_{y_{n}}(|w_{n}|^{2}w_{n}))\,ds.

By Lemma
5.2
, we first observe that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert N_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1}([0,\tau])}=0,

giving an acceptable estimate for
N_{1}
.

For
N_{2}
, we will use the interpolation estimate

\left\lVert N_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{1}}([0,\tau])}\lesssim\left\lVert N_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{0}([0,\tau])}^{1-\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}}}\left\lVert N_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{2}}([0,\tau])}^{\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}}}.

Using the Strichartz estimates for
\mathcal{L}(t,0;z_{n})
and
e^{-it\Delta}
, we first observe that

\left\lVert N_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{2}}([0,\tau])}\lesssim\left\lVert|w_{n}|^{2}w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{\theta_{1}}_{6/5}([0,\tau])}\lesssim\left\lVert w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{4}L^{6}([0,\tau])}^{2}\left\lVert w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}H^{\theta_{2}}([0,\tau])}\lesssim A^{3}.

We next write

\displaystyle N_{2}=iT_{y_{n}}^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}

\displaystyle\biggl[\mathcal{L}(t,s;z_{n})(V(T_{y_{n}}d_{n})

\displaystyle\quad-B[z_{n}](T_{y_{n}}d_{n})+(1-P_{c})T_{y_{n}}|w_{n}|^{2}w_{n}))(s)\biggr]\,ds,

where

d_{n}(t)=-i\sigma\int_{0}^{t}e^{-i(t-s)\Delta}(|w_{n}|^{2}w_{n})(s)\,ds

solves

(i{\partial}_{t}-\Delta)d_{n}=\sigma|w_{n}|^{2}w_{n},\quad d_{n}(0)=0.

We consider each term in
N_{2}
separately. First, by Strichartz, we have that

\left\lVert d_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{2}}}\lesssim\left\lVert|w_{n}|^{2}w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}H^{\theta_{2}}_{6/5}}\lesssim\left\lVert w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{4}L^{6}}^{2}\left\lVert w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}H^{\theta_{2}}}\lesssim A^{3}.

Next, we observe that

\displaystyle\left\lvert{\partial}_{t}\int\chi(\tfrac{\cdot}{R})|d_{n}|^{2}\,dx\right\rvert

\displaystyle{}\lesssim R^{-1}\left\lVert d_{n}\right\rVert^{2}_{H^{1/2}}+\left\lVert d_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{6}}\left\lVert\chi(\tfrac{\cdot}{R})w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{6}}^{2}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim R^{-1}+\left\lVert d_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{6}}\left\lVert\chi(\tfrac{\cdot}{R})w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{6}}^{2}.

In particular, using (
6.28
), given
\varepsilon>0
, we have that

\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\int\chi(\tfrac{\cdot}{R})|d_{n}|^{2}\,dx\lesssim\tau R^{-1}+\tau^{\frac{1}{4}}A^{5}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\chi(\tfrac{\cdot}{R})w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}L^{2}([0,\tau])}\lesssim\varepsilon

for
R
sufficiently large. From this estimate, we can deduce that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert VT_{y_{n}}d_{n}\right\rVert_{(L^{1}L^{2}+L^{2}L^{\frac{6}{5}})([0,\tau])}=0,

which suffices to treat the first term appearing in the formula for
N_{2}
above. Here the
L^{1}L^{2}
and
L^{2}L^{\frac{6}{5}}
norms are used to estimate the
L^{2}
and
L^{\infty}
parts of
V
, respectively.

For the second term appearing in the formula for
N_{2}
, we note that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert B[z_{n}](T_{y_{n}}d_{n})\right\rVert_{L^{1}L^{2}([0,\tau])}=0,

which follows from the fact that
z_{n}
takes values in a compact set of
\mathbb{C}
.

Finally, for the third term in the formula for
N_{2}
, we note that by (
6.28
) implies

\left\lVert(1-P_{c})T_{y_{n}}|w_{n}|^{2}w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{1}L^{2}([0,\tau])}\leqslant\tau\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}|(T_{y_{n}}^{-1}\phi_{0},|w_{n}|^{2}w_{n})|\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

Combining the estimates above, we obtain

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert N_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{0}([0,\tau])}=0,\quad\text{and hence}\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert N_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{1}}([0,\tau])}=0.

Finally let us estimate
N_{3}
. We begin by writing

\displaystyle\tilde{N}(z_{n},T_{y_{n}}w_{n})

\displaystyle-\sigma P_{c}T_{y_{n}}(|w_{n}|^{2}w_{n})

(6.31)

\displaystyle=\sigma P_{c}(2\Phi[z_{n}]|R[z_{n}]T_{y_{n}}w_{n}|^{2}+\overline{\Phi[z_{n}]}(R[z_{n}]T_{y_{n}}w_{n})^{2})

\displaystyle+\sigma P_{c}(|R[z_{n}]T_{y_{n}}w_{n}|^{2}R[z_{n}]T_{y_{n}}w_{n}-|T_{y_{n}}w_{n}|^{2}T_{y_{n}}w_{n})

\displaystyle-iP_{c}D\Phi[z_{n}]\underline{N}(z_{n},R[z_{n}]T_{y_{n}}w_{n}))

and observing that this is a sum of multilinear functions with respect to
w_{n}
. By a density argument, we may approximate
w_{n}
on
[0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
by a smooth function in the
\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{1}}([0,\tau])
norm. In particular, the error is small in the
L^{1}H^{\theta_{1}}
-norm, so thatit suffices to estimate the term above in
L^{1}H^{1}
assuming that
w_{n}
is smooth.

We next observe that

|(R[z_{n}]-1)T_{y_{n}}w_{n}|\leqslant|c_{n}||\phi_{0}|

with
|c_{n}|\lesssim|(\Phi[z_{n}],T_{y_{n}}w_{n})|\to 0
as
n\to\infty
uniformly in
t
. Thus
R[z_{n}]
may be replaced by the identity with an acceptable error.

The quadratic part (the first line on the right-hand side of (
6.31
)) is small in
L^{1}H^{1}
since each term (along with their derivatives) contains a product
\Phi[z_{n}]\cdot T_{y_{n}}w_{n}
or
\nabla\Phi[z_{n}]\cdot T_{y_{n}}w_{n}
, which tend to zero in
L_{t}^{\infty}(L^{1}\cap L^{2})_{x}([0,\tau])
as
n\to\infty
(cf. (
6.28
)).

The cubic part (the second line on the right-hand side of (
6.31
)) is acceptable, as it is essentially
\mathcal{O}(|(R[z_{n}]-1)T_{y_{n}}w_{n}||T_{y_{n}}w_{n}|^{2})
. The last line on the right-hand side of (
6.31
) is handled similarly.

Thus we obtain that as
n\to\infty
, we have

\left\lVert N_{3}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{1}}([0,\tau])}\lesssim\left\lVert\tilde{N}(z_{n},T_{y_{n}}w_{n})-\sigma P_{c}T_{y_{n}}(|w_{n}|^{2}w_{n})\right\rVert_{L^{1}H^{\theta_{1}}([0,\tau])}\to 0.

As the same argument handles negative times, we complete the proof of (
6.29
).∎

Proof of Proposition
6.17
.

We prove the bound for positive times. We fix
\tau>0
and let
\varepsilon_{0},\delta_{0}>0
be constants to be chosen later. To simplify notation, we omit superscripts and denote
v_{n}=\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}
and
w_{\infty}=w_{\infty}^{(0,k)}
. We also define

w_{n}=(T_{y_{n}})^{-1}v_{n},\quad\text{where}\quad y_{n}\quad\text{denotes}\quad y_{n}^{(0,k)}.

As
M:=\left\lVert w_{\infty}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty
, there exists
L_{0}\leqslant 1+(M/\delta_{0})^{1/8}
and a partition
\{t_{\ell}\}_{\ell=0}^{L_{0}}
of
[0,\tau]
such that

\sup_{\ell\in[1,L_{0}]}\left\lVert w_{\infty}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([t_{\ell-1},t_{\ell}])}\leqslant\delta_{0}.

We now choose
\theta_{\ell}
so that

1=\theta_{1}>\theta_{2}>\dots>\theta_{L_{0}}\geqslant\tfrac{3}{4}.

We will show by induction on
k\in[0,L_{0}]
that there exists
N_{k}\in\mathbb{N}
, increasing
C_{k}>0
(increasing in
k
), and
\varepsilon_{k}>0
(decreasing in
k
) such that if

\left\lVert w_{n}(0)-w_{\infty}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{k},

then

\left\lVert w_{n}-w_{\infty}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{k}}([0,t_{k}])}\leqslant\min\{C_{k}\left\lVert w_{n}(0)-w_{\infty}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{1}},\varepsilon_{0}\}\quad\text{for}\quad n\geqslant N_{k}.

(6.32)

The base case
k=0
is straightforward for any choice of
\varepsilon_{0}>0
. Indeed, since

w_{n}(0)-w_{\infty}(0)=(P_{c}-1)T_{y_{n}}\varphi^{(0,k)}=\phi_{0}(T_{y_{n}}\varphi^{(0,k)},\phi_{0})\to 0

in
H^{1}
as
n\to\infty
, the assumption is satisfied if
n
is sufficiently large.

Now suppose that we have (
6.32
) at some
k_{0}\in[0,L_{0}-1]
. Then, provided
\delta_{0}>0
is sufficiently small, we have that

\left\lVert e^{-i(t-t_{k_{0}})\Delta}w(t_{k_{0}})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([t_{k_{0}},t_{k_{0}+1}])}\leqslant 2\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([t_{k_{0}},t_{k_{0}+1}])}\leqslant 2\delta_{0}.

By Lemma
5.2
, this implies

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,t_{k_{0}};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}w_{\infty}(t_{k_{0}})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([t_{k_{0}},t_{k_{0}+1}])}\leqslant 3\delta_{0}

for large
n
. Consequently, by Strichartz and the inductive hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}(\cdot,

\displaystyle t_{k_{0}};z_{n}){w_{n}}(t_{k_{0}})\|_{L^{8}L^{4}([t_{k_{0}},t_{k_{0}+1}])}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,t_{k_{0}};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}w_{\infty}(t_{k_{0}})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([t_{k_{0}},t_{k_{0}+1}])}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,t_{k_{0}};z_{n})P_{c}T_{y_{n}}(w_{\infty}-w_{n})(t_{k_{0}})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([t_{k_{0}},t_{k_{0}+1}])}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{0}+\left\lVert(w_{\infty}-w_{n})(t_{k_{0}})\right\rVert_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\delta_{0}+\varepsilon_{0}

for large
n
. We now fix
\delta_{0}
and
\varepsilon_{0}
so small that Lemma
4.11
implies

\displaystyle\left\lVert v_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([t_{k_{0}},t_{k_{0}+1}])}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,t_{k_{0}};z_{n})v_{n}(t_{k_{0}})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([t_{k_{0}},t_{k_{0}+1}])}\lesssim\delta_{0}+\varepsilon_{0},

\displaystyle\left\lVert v_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{k_{0}}}([t_{k_{0}},t_{k_{0}+1}])}

\displaystyle{}\leqslant C(\left\lVert v_{n}(t_{k_{0}})\right\rVert_{H^{\theta_{k_{0}}}}+\sup_{n}\left\lVert z_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}})

\displaystyle\leqslant C(\left\lVert w_{\infty}(t_{k_{0}})\right\rVert_{H^{\theta_{k_{0}}}}+\delta_{0}+\sup_{n}\left\lVert z_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}})

\displaystyle\leqslant 2C(\left\lVert w_{\infty}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}+\sup_{n}\left\lVert z_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}})=:A.

Furthermore, since
v_{n}(0)\in H^{1}
, we have
v_{n}(t_{k_{0}})\in H^{1}
. Then, by Lemma
6.18
(i.e. (
6.28
) and (
6.29
)), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert v_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}L^{2}([t_{k_{0}},t_{k_{0}+1}]\times\{|x-y_{n}|\geqslant R\})}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}(i{\partial}_{t}v_{n}-\Delta v_{n}-\sigma|v_{n}|^{2}v_{n})\,ds\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{k_{0}+1}}([t_{k_{0}},t_{k_{0}+1}])}

\displaystyle=0.

Then, together with

\displaystyle\left\lVert(w_{\infty}-w_{n})(t_{k_{0}})\right\rVert_{H^{\theta_{k_{0}+1}}}\leqslant\left\lVert(w_{\infty}-w_{n})(t_{k_{0}})\right\rVert_{H^{\theta_{k_{0}}}}\leqslant C_{k_{0}}\left\lVert w_{n}(0)-w_{\infty}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\left\lVert w_{\infty}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([t_{k_{0}},t_{k_{0}+1}])}\leqslant\delta_{0},

we see from perturbation around
w_{\infty}
that

\left\lVert w_{\infty}-w_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{k_{0}+1}}([t_{k_{0}},t_{k_{0}+1}])}\lesssim\left\lVert w_{n}(0)-w_{\infty}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{1}}.

Hence, there exists
C_{k_{0}+1}\geqslant C_{k_{0}}
such that

\left\lVert w_{\infty}-w_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{\theta_{k_{0}+1}}([0,t_{k_{0}+1}])}\leqslant C_{k_{0}+1}\left\lVert w_{n}(0)-w_{\infty}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{1}}.

Setting
\varepsilon_{k_{0}+1}:=\varepsilon_{0}/C_{k_{0}+1}
, we obtain (
6.32
) for large
n
under the assumption that
\left\lVert w_{n}(0)-w_{\infty}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{k_{0}+1}
.

By induction, we have that (
6.32
) holds for
k=L_{0}
. Thus

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert w_{n}-w_{\infty}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{3/4}([0,\tau])}=0

(6.33)

for any
\tau>0
, which yields (
6.26
). Furthermore, by (
6.28
),

\lim_{R\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}([0,\tau]\times\{|x|\geqslant R\})}=0

(6.34)

for any
\tau>0
. Interpolating (
6.33
) with (
6.34
), we obtain

\lim_{R\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}([-\tau,\tau]\times\{|x|\geqslant R\})}=0.

(6.35)

To complete the proof, it suffices to show (
6.25
). Indeed, (
6.24
) follows from (
6.25
) and (
6.35
). Similarly, (
6.23
) follows from (
6.25
), (
6.33
), and the fact that
w_{\infty}\in L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})
.

We turn to (
6.25
) and let
\varepsilon>0
. Using (
5.17
), we find that there exists
\tau_{0}=\tau_{0}(\varepsilon)>0
such that if
\tau\geqslant\tau_{0}
, then

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau;z_{n})w_{\infty}(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n}(\cdot+\tau))w_{\infty}(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([0,\infty))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert e^{-it\Delta}w_{\infty}(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([0,\infty))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert e^{-i(t-\tau)\Delta}w_{\infty}(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert w_{\infty}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}\lesssim\varepsilon.

On the other hand, using (
6.33
), we have that for each
\tau\geqslant\tau_{0}
, there exists
N=N(\tau)
such that if
n\geqslant N
, then

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau;z_{n})(w_{n}-w_{\infty}(\tau))\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}\lesssim\left\lVert w_{n}-w_{\infty}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{3/4}([0,\tau])}\leqslant\varepsilon.

For such
\tau
and
n
, we therefore have that

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau;z_{n})w_{n}(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}\lesssim\varepsilon.

If
\varepsilon
is small then by stability (Lemma
4.12
), we have that
w_{n}
is forward global and satisfies

\left\lVert w_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}\leqslant 2\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau;z_{n})w_{n}(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}\lesssim\varepsilon.

As the same argument applies for negative times, we derive (
6.25
).
∎

We next bound the
sum
of nonlinear profiles of the form
\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}
:

Lemma 6.19
.

We have the following bounds:

\displaystyle\sup_{K\geqslant 1}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty,

(6.36)

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\sup_{K\geqslant 1}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}((-\infty,-\tau]\cup[\tau,\infty))}=0.

(6.37)

Proof.

We begin with (
6.36
). If
k_{1}\neq k_{2}
then we see from (
6.23
), (
6.24
), and orthogonality of the profiles that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\lVert\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k_{1})}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k_{2})}\rVert_{L^{4}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}=0.

Thus

\displaystyle\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{4}

\displaystyle=\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in[1,K]}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k_{1})}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k_{2})}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k_{3})}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k_{4})}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}L^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle{}\leqslant\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\lVert\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{4}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\lVert w_{\infty}^{k}\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{4}.

As
\left\lVert\varphi_{\infty}^{k}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
, the small-data theory yields sufficiently large
K_{*}
that

k\geq K_{*}\implies\lVert w_{\infty}^{k}\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}\leqslant 2\lVert\varphi^{(0,k)}\rVert_{H^{1}}.

It follows that

\sup_{K\geqslant 1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\lVert w_{\infty}^{k}\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{4}\leqslant\sum_{k=1}^{K_{*}}\lVert w_{\infty}^{k}\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{4}+2^{4}\sum_{k=K_{*}+1}^{\infty}\lVert\varphi^{(0,k)}\rVert_{H^{1}}^{4}<\infty,

which gives (
6.36
).

We turn to (
6.37
). We let
\varepsilon>0
and argue as above to deduce the existence of
K_{0}\geqslant K_{*}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{K\geqslant K_{0}}

\displaystyle\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{k=K_{0}}^{K}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup_{K\geqslant K_{0}}\sum_{k=K_{0}}^{K}\lVert w_{\infty}^{k}\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{4}\leqslant 2^{4}\sum_{k=K_{0}}^{\infty}\lVert\varphi^{(0,k)}\rVert_{H^{1}}^{4}\leqslant\varepsilon.

For this
K_{0}
, we see from (
6.25
) that there exists
\tau_{0}
such that
\tau\geqslant\tau_{0}
implies

\displaystyle\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}((-\infty,-\tau]\cup[\tau,\infty)))}

\displaystyle\leqslant K_{0}\sup_{k\in[1,K_{0}]}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}((-\infty,-\tau]\cup[\tau,\infty)))}\leqslant\varepsilon.

Combining these two estimates, we obtain the result.
∎

6.3.3.
Estimates on
\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}

We next consider nonlinear profiles
\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}
with
s\in[1,s_{\max}]
. We let
w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}
be the solution to the following cubic NLS:

\begin{cases}&i{\partial}_{t}w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}-\Delta w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}=\sigma\bigl|w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\bigr|^{2}w_{\infty}^{(s,k)},\\
&w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}(-\tau)=e^{i\tau\Delta}{\varphi^{(s,k)}},\end{cases}

(6.38)

where we denote the profile
\varphi_{\infty}^{j}
appearing in Definition
6.6
by
\varphi^{(s,k)}.
We first collect some properties of
w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}
.

Lemma 6.20
.

Suppose that (
3.16
) holds. Let
s\in[1,s_{\max}]
and
k\geqslant 1
. If
\varphi^{(s,k)}\neq 0
then
\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{(s,k)})\leqslant M_{*}
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{0}(\varphi^{(s,k)})\leqslant\min\bigl\{E_{*}-\mathscr{E}_{0}(M_{*}),\tfrac{1}{\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{(s,k)})}\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{E}_{0}(Q)-\delta_{0}\bigr\}.

In particular, for any
\tau>0
there exists a unique global solution
w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\in C(\mathbb{R},H^{1})
to (
6.38
) such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau>0}\left\lVert w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim_{\delta_{0}}1,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau>0}\left\lVert w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\lesssim\mathbb{H}_{0}(\varphi^{(s,k)})+\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{(s,k)}).

Furthermore, writing
w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)}
for the unique global solution to

\begin{cases}i{\partial}_{t}w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)}-\Delta w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)}=\sigma\bigl|w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)}\bigr|^{2}w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)},\\
\displaystyle\lim_{t\to-\infty}e^{it\Delta}w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)}(t)=\varphi^{(s,k)},\end{cases}

(6.39)

we have

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\left\lVert w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}-w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}=0.

(6.40)

Proof.

By (
5.6
) and (
6.22
), we have

E_{*}\geqslant\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{\infty}^{0})+\mathbb{H}_{0}(\varphi^{(s,k)}).

In particular,
\mathbb{H}_{0}(\varphi^{(s,k)})\leqslant E_{*}-\mathscr{E}_{0}(M_{*})
.

Next, by (
5.2
) and (
5.18
), one obtains

M_{*}\geqslant\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{0})+\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{(s,k)})

Thus, mimicking the proof of (
6.20
), we obtain

\mathbb{H}_{0}(\varphi^{(s,k)})\leqslant\frac{1}{\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{(s,k)})}\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{E}_{0}(Q)-\delta_{0}.

Now, for any
\tau>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}(e^{i\tau\Delta}\varphi^{(s,k)})\mathbb{E}_{0}(e^{i\tau\Delta}\varphi^{(s,k)})<\mathbb{M}(e^{i\tau\Delta}\varphi^{(s,k)})\mathbb{H}_{0}(e^{i\tau\Delta}\varphi^{(s,k)})<\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{E}_{0}(Q),

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}(e^{i\tau\Delta}\varphi^{(s,k)})\mathbb{H}_{0}(e^{i\tau\Delta}\varphi^{(s,k)})<\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{E}_{0}(Q)<\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{H}_{0}(Q).

Thus, one sees from the scattering theory for the cubic NLS (cf.
[
6
]
) theory that
w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}
exists globally in time and satisfies the bounds

\displaystyle\left\lVert w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim_{\delta_{0}}1,

\displaystyle\left\lVert w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{0}(e^{i\tau\Delta}\varphi^{(s,k)})+\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{(s,k)}).

Let
w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)}
be the solution to (
6.39
). Since this solution scatters to
\varphi^{(s,k)}
backward in time, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}(w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)})=\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{(s,k)}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}(w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)})=\mathbb{H}_{0}(\varphi^{(s,k)}),

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to-\infty}\mathbb{K}_{0,2}(w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)})=2\mathbb{H}_{0}(\varphi^{(s,k)})>0.

These imply that

\mathbb{M}(w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)})\mathbb{E}_{0}(w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)})<\mathbb{M}(Q)\mathbb{H}_{0}(Q),

which guarantees scattering forward in time as well, with the bounds

\displaystyle\left\lVert w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim 1,

\displaystyle\left\lVert w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{0}(\varphi^{(s,k)})+\mathbb{M}(\varphi^{(s,k)}).

The convergence
w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\to w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)}
then follows from a standard stability argument. In particular, the convergence implies that the bound on
w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}
is uniform in
\tau>0
.
∎

We can then prove the following properties for the nonlinear profiles
\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}
. As above, we write
y_{n}
for the sequence of translations
y_{n}^{(s,k)}
.

Proposition 6.21
.

Suppose that (
3.16
) holds. Let
k\geqslant 1
, and define
\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}
be as in Definition
6.6
. Let
w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\in C(\mathbb{R},H^{1})
be the unique global solution to (
6.38
) given in Lemma
6.20
. Then, for any
\tau>0
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s})-T_{y_{n}}w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}=0,

(6.41)

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert{\bf 1}_{\{|x-y_{n}|\geqslant R\}}\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}=0.

(6.42)

In particular,

\sup_{\tau>0}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty.

(6.43)

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to\infty}

\displaystyle\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\biggl\{\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,-\tau+s_{n}^{s};z_{n})\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}(-\tau+s_{n}^{s})\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}((-\infty,-\tau+s_{n}^{s}))}

(6.44)

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau+s_{n}^{s};z_{n})\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}(\tau+s_{n}^{s})\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}((\tau,\infty))}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}((-\infty,-\tau+s_{n}^{s})\cup(\tau+s_{n}^{s},\infty))}\biggr\}=0.

Proof.

Using the fact that
\lVert w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty
, the proof is essentially the same as that of (
6.17
). Next, define

w_{n}^{(s,k)}(t)=T_{y_{n}^{(s,k)}}^{-1}\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}(t+s_{n}^{s}).

Then, mimicking the proof of (
6.33
) and (
6.35
), we may obtain

\lim_{n\to\infty}\biggl\{\left\lVert w_{n}^{(s,k)}-w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{3/4}([-\tau^{\prime},-\tau])}+\left\lVert w_{n}^{(s,k)}-w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{3/4}([-\tau,\tau^{\prime}])}\biggr\}=0

(6.45)

and

\lim_{R\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert w_{n}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([-\tau^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}]\times\{|x|\geqslant R\})}=0

for any
\tau^{\prime}\geqslant\tau>0
. Then, (
6.41
) and (
6.42
) follow from

\lim_{\tau^{\prime}\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\biggl\{\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,-\tau^{\prime};z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s}))\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}(-\tau^{\prime})\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}((-\infty,-\tau^{\prime}))}\\
+\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau^{\prime};z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s}))\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}(\tau^{\prime})\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}((\tau^{\prime},\infty))}\biggr\}=0,

(6.46)

which we deduce from
\lVert w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty
as in the proof of (
6.25
). Note that
\tau^{\prime}
is not the same parameter as
\tau
in (
6.46
) (i.e.
\tau
is fixed in (
6.46
)). We then obtain (
6.43
) from (
6.41
) and the bound

\sup_{\tau>0}\lVert w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty.

We turn to (
6.44
). By the small data theory, it suffices to establish the following:

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,-\tau;z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s}))\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}(-\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}((-\infty,-\tau))}=0,

(6.47)

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau;z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s}))\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}_{t}L^{4}_{x}((\tau,\infty))}=0.

(6.48)

Note that by definition, we have

\mathcal{L}(t-\tau,-\tau;z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s}))\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}(-\tau)=\mathcal{L}(t;0;z_{n}(\cdot-\tau+s_{n}^{s}))P_{c}T_{y_{n}}e^{i\tau\Delta}\varphi^{(s,k)}.

Thus, one sees from (
5.17
) that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(t-\tau,-\tau;z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s}))\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}(-\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}((-\infty,0])}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim\left\lVert e^{-i\cdot\Delta}(e^{i\tau\Delta}\varphi^{(s,k)})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}((-\infty,0])}

\displaystyle{}=\left\lVert e^{-i\cdot\Delta}\varphi^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}((-\infty,-\tau])}.

As this term tends to zero as
\tau\to\infty
(and is independent of
n
), we obtain (
6.47
).

For (
6.48
), we pick
\varepsilon>0
and choose
\tau_{0}
such that if
\tau^{\prime}>\tau_{0}
, then

\left\lVert w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}([\tau,\infty))}\leqslant\varepsilon.

Then we see from (
6.40
) that there exists
\tau_{1}\geqslant\tau_{0}
such that if
\tau>\tau_{1}
, then

\displaystyle\left\lVert w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}([\tau,\infty))}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}([\tau,\infty))}+\left\lVert w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}-w_{\infty,-}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Stz}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\leqslant 2\varepsilon.

Thus, if
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small, the small-data theory implies that

\left\lVert e^{-i(\cdot-\tau)\Delta}w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}([\tau,\infty))}\leqslant 4\varepsilon.

Using (
5.17
), we obtain

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n}(\cdot+\tau+s_{n}^{s}))w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}\cap L^{2}H^{1/2}_{6}([0,\infty))}\lesssim\varepsilon,

which implies

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau;z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s}))w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}\lesssim\varepsilon.

(6.49)

On the other hand, by the uniform Strichartz estimate (Proposition
4.6
),

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau;z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s}))(w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}-w_{n}^{(s,k)})(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}\lesssim\left\lVert(w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}-w_{n}^{(s,k)})(\tau)\right\rVert_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Thus, in light of (
6.45
), there exists
N(\tau)
such that if
n\geqslant N
, we have

\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau;z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s}))(w_{\infty}^{(s,k)}-w_{n}^{(s,k)})(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}\leqslant\varepsilon.

(6.50)

Combining (
6.49
) and (
6.50
), we see that

\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau;z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s}))w_{n}^{(s,k)}(\tau)\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty))}\lesssim\varepsilon

for any
\tau\geqslant\tau_{1}
, which yields (
6.48
).∎

Once again, we can derive bounds on the
sum
of nonlinear profiles of the form
\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}
. As the proof is essentially the same as that of Lemma
6.19
, we omit it.

Lemma 6.22
.

We have the following bounds:

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau>0}\sup_{K\geqslant 1}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty,

(6.51)

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\sup_{K\geqslant 1}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}((-\infty,-\tau+s_{n}^{s}]\cup[\tau+s_{n}^{s},\infty)))}=0.

(6.52)

6.4.
Completion of the proof

Finally, we turn to the proof of Proposition
6.8
, which we will then use to complete the proof of Proposition
3.7
.

Proof of Proposition
6.8
.

We consider the positive time direction only. We suppose towards a contradiction that

(\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{s}),\mathbb{E}_{V}(u_{\infty}^{s}))\neq(M_{*},E_{*})

for all
s\in[0,s_{\max}]
. Ultimately, we will prove that

\|\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau+s_{n}^{s_{\max}};z_{n})[(\xi_{n}-\Gamma_{n}^{J})(\tau+s_{n}^{s_{\max}})]\|_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau+s_{n}^{s_{\max}},\infty))}\to 0

(6.53)

as
n,J,\tau\to\infty
, from which one can deduce scattering of
\xi_{n}
as
t\to\infty
(and hence reach a contradiction). The proof will rely on an induction argument. In particular, for each
s_{0}\in[0,s_{\max}]
, we define approximations

\displaystyle\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{s_{0},J}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=0}^{K(s_{0},J)}\Lambda_{n}^{(s_{0},k)}+\sum_{s=s_{0}+1}^{s_{\max}}\sum_{k=0}^{K(s,J)}\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}+\Gamma_{n}^{J},

(6.54)

\displaystyle\hat{\xi}_{n}^{s_{0},J}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{s=s_{0}}^{s_{\max}}\sum_{k=0}^{K(s,J)}\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}+\Gamma_{n}^{J},

(6.55)

(see (
6.4
) for the definition of
K(s,J
)) and introduce the notation

\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}}=\tau+s_{n}^{s_{0}}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}}=-\tau+s_{n}^{s_{0}}.

(6.56)

Note that for fixed
\tau>0
, we have
\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}}<\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}}<\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}+1}
for all
n
large. We will prove by induction that for each
s_{0}\in[0,s_{\max}]
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau>0}\lim_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}};z_{n})[(\xi_{n}-\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{s_{0},J})(\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}})]\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}},\infty])}=0,

(6.57)

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\varlimsup_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}};z_{n})[(\xi_{n}-\hat{\xi}_{n}^{s_{0},J})(\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}})]\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}},\infty))}=0.

(6.58)

More precisely, we will prove:

(i)

(
6.57
) holds for
s_{0}=0
;

(ii)

(
6.57
) at level
s_{0}<s_{\max}
implies (
6.58
) at level
s_{0}+1
;

(iii)

(
6.58
) at level
s_{0}\in[1,s_{\max}]
implies (
6.57
) at level
s_{0}
; and

(iv)

(
6.57
) at level
s_{\max}
implies (
6.53
).

Recall that once we have (
6.53
), we complete the proof of Proposition
6.8
. In particular, if
s_{\max}=0
, then it is only necessary to prove (i) and (iv). Thus, in what follows we assume that
s_{\max}\geq 1
.

(i) Estimate (
6.57
) for
s_{0}=0
.
Define
\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{0,J}
as in (
6.54
) (with
s_{0}=0
), as well as the corresponding error

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{0,n}

\displaystyle{}:=(i{\partial}_{t}+H-B[z_{n}])\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{0,J}-\tilde{N}(z_{n},\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{0,J})

\displaystyle{}=-(B[z_{n}]-B[z_{\infty}^{0}])\xi_{\infty}^{0}+[\tilde{N}(z_{\infty}^{0},\xi_{\infty}^{0})-\tilde{N}(z_{n},\xi_{\infty}^{0})]

\displaystyle{}\quad-\bigl[\tilde{N}(z_{n},\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{0,J})-\sum_{k=0}^{K(0,J)}\tilde{N}(z_{n},\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)})-\tilde{N}(z_{n},\Gamma_{n}^{J})\bigr].

We claim (to be proven below) that

\sup_{\tau>0}\lim_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\bigl\|\tilde{N}(z_{n},\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{0,J})-\!\!\sum_{k=0}^{K(0,J)}\tilde{N}(z_{n},\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)})-\tilde{N}(z_{n},\Gamma_{n}^{J})\bigr\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}L^{\frac{4}{3}}([-\tau,\tau])}=0.

(6.59)

By the local uniform convergence
z_{n}\to z_{\infty}^{0}
, we also have that

\left\lVert(B[z_{n}]-B[z_{\infty}^{0}])\xi_{\infty}^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{1}H^{1}([-\tau,\tau])}+\left\lVert\tilde{N}(z_{\infty}^{0},\xi_{\infty}^{0})-\tilde{N}(z_{n},\xi_{\infty}^{0})\right\rVert_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}L^{\frac{4}{3}}([-\tau,\tau])}\to 0

as
n\to\infty
, for each
\tau>0
. Thus,

\sup_{\tau>0}\lim_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{D}(\cdot,0;z_{n})({\bf 1}_{[0,\tau]}\mathcal{E}_{0,n})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([0,\infty))}=0

(cf. (
4.2
) for the definition of
\mathcal{D}
).

Now, by definition, we have

\xi_{n}(0)-\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{0,J}(0)=\sum_{s=s_{\min}}^{-1}\sum_{k=0}^{K(s,J)}\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)},

so that Lemma
6.3
(along with the fact that
s_{n}^{0}\equiv 0
) implies that

\sup_{\tau>0}\lim_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,0;z_{n})(\xi_{n}(0)-\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{0,J}(0))\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([0,\tau])}=0.

(6.60)

Thus, by stability, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau>0}\lim_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\xi_{n}-\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{0,J}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([0,\tau])}=0,

(6.61)

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau>0}\lim_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\xi_{n}-\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{0,J}\right\rVert_{[z_{n};0,\tau;\infty]}=0

(6.62)

(see (
4.7
) for the semi-norm notation). Using this together with (
6.60
), we also obtain

\sup_{\tau>0}\lim_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau;z_{n})(\xi_{n}(\tau)-\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{0,J}(\tau))\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tau,\infty])}=0,

which is the desired estimate (
6.57
) for
s_{0}=0
. It remains to establish (
6.59
).

Proof of (
6.59
).

Let
\tau>0
. We prove the estimate on
[0,\tau]
; similar arguments treat the interval
[-\tau,0]
. Noting that by construction, we have

\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{0,J}-\sum_{s=1}^{s_{\max}}\sum_{k=0}^{K(s,J)}\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}-\Gamma_{n}^{J}=\sum_{k=0}^{K(0,J)}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)},

we first estimate

\displaystyle\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\biggl\|\tilde{N}(z_{n},\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{0,J})-\tilde{N}\bigl(z_{n},\sum_{k=0}^{K(0,J)}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\bigr)\biggr\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}L^{\frac{4}{3}}({[0,\tau]})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\biggl[\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\bigl\|\sum_{k=0}^{K(0,J)}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\bigr\|_{L^{8}L^{4}([0,\tau])}\biggr]^{2}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\bigl\|\sum_{s=1}^{s_{\max}}\sum_{k=0}^{K(s,J)}\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}+\Gamma_{n}^{J}\bigr\|_{L^{8}L^{4}([0,\tau])}.

Now, by Proposition
6.14
, Lemma
6.19
, and the assumption that
\mathbb{M}(u_{\infty}^{0})<M_{*}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{J\geqslant J^{\dagger}+1}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\biggl\|

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K(0,J)}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\biggr\|_{L^{8}L^{4}([0,\tau])}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\xi_{\infty}^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}+\sup_{K\geqslant 1}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([0,\tau])}<\infty.

On the other hand, we see from Lemma
6.3
that

\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{s=1}^{s_{\max}}\sum_{k=0}^{K(s,J)}\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}+\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([0,\tau])}=\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([0,\tau])}.

Thus, in view of (
6.11
), we have

\sup_{\tau>0}\lim_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\tilde{N}(z_{n},\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{0,J})-\tilde{N}(z_{n},{\sum_{k=0}^{K(0,J)}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)})}\right\rVert_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}L^{\frac{4}{3}}([0,\tau])}=0,

and similarly

\sup_{\tau>0}\lim_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\tilde{N}(z_{n},\Gamma_{n}^{J})\right\rVert_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}L^{\frac{4}{3}}([0,\tau])}=0.

Finally, using (
6.23
), (
6.24
), and
the fact that
\Lambda_{n}^{(0,0)}=\xi_{\infty}^{0}\in L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})
satisfies

\lim_{R\to\infty}\sup_{n}\left\lVert{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\geqslant R\}}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,0)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}(\mathbb{R})}=0,

we can estimate

\lim_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\bigl\|\tilde{N}\bigl(z_{n},\sum_{k=0}^{K(0,J)}\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)}\bigr)-\sum_{k=0}^{K(0,J)}\tilde{N}(z_{n},\Lambda_{n}^{(0,k)})\bigr\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}L^{\frac{4}{3}}([0,\tau])}=0.

Collecting the estimates, we obtain (
6.59
).∎

(ii) Estimate (
6.57
) at level
s_{0}
implies estimate (
6.58
) at level
s_{0}+1
.
Suppose that (
6.57
) holds at level
s_{0}<s_{\max}
. As every profile such that
s(j)=s_{0}
scatters forward in time, we have

\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left(\left\lVert\Lambda_{n}^{(s_{0},k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}},\infty))}+\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tau;z_{n})\Lambda_{n}^{(s_{0},k)}(\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}},\infty))}\right)=0

(6.63)

as
\tau\to\infty
for all
k
. Using (
6.37
), we also obtain

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\sup_{J\geqslant J^{\dagger}+1}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{K(s_{0},J)}\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}};z_{n})\Lambda_{n}^{(s_{0},k)}(\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}},\infty))}=0.

(6.64)

Inserting (
6.63
) and (
6.64
) into (
6.57
), we derive

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\varlimsup_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\bigl\|\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}};z_{n})\bigl(\xi_{n}-\hat{\xi}_{n}^{s_{0}+1,J}\bigr)(\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}})\bigr\|_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}},\infty))}=0,

(6.65)

where
\hat{\xi}_{n}^{s_{0}+1,J}
is as in (
6.55
).

We need to upgrade (
6.65
) to obtain (
6.58
) at level
s_{0}+1
. To this end, we first observe that

\mathcal{L}(t,\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}};z_{n})\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}(\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}})=\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}(t).

Then, by Lemma
6.3
, we have

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\sup_{J\geqslant J^{\dagger}+1}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{s\geqslant s_{0}+1}\sum_{k=0}^{K(s,J)}\lambda_{n}^{(s,k)}\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}},\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}+1}])}=0,

(6.66)

which (together with (
6.65
)) yields

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\varlimsup_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}};z_{n})\left(\xi_{n}-\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right)(\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}},\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}+1}])}=0.

(6.67)

Since
\xi_{n}
and
\Gamma_{n}^{j}
solve the nonlinear equation

i\partial_{t}v+Hv=B[z_{n}]v+\tilde{N}(z_{n},v),

the stability theory (Lemma
4.12
) yields

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\varlimsup_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\xi_{n}-\Gamma_{n}^{J}\right\rVert_{[z_{n};\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}},\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}+1};\infty]}=0.

In particular,

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\varlimsup_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\|\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}+1};z_{n})(\xi_{n}-\Gamma_{n}^{J})(\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}+1})\\
-\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}};z_{n})(\xi_{n}-\Gamma_{n}^{J})(\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}})\|_{L^{8}L^{4}([\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}+1},\infty))}=0.

(6.68)

By (
6.66
) and (
6.68
), we have

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\varlimsup_{J\to\infty}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}+1};z_{n})(\xi_{n}-\hat{\xi}_{n}^{s_{0}+1,J})(\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}+1})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}+1},\infty))}=0,

(6.69)

which is (
6.58
) at level
s_{0}+1
, as desired.

(iii) Estimate (
6.58
) at level
s_{0}
implies estimate (
6.57
) at level
s_{0}
.
Suppose that (
6.58
) holds at level
s_{0}\in[1,s_{\max}]
, and let
\tau>0
.

Note that by construction, we may write

\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{s_{0},J}=\hat{\xi}_{n}^{s_{0},J}+\sum_{k=0}^{K(s_{0},J)}(\Lambda_{n}^{(s_{0},k)}-\lambda_{n}^{(s_{0},k)})

(6.70)

(cf. (
6.54
) and (
6.55
)). We now let
\chi\in C_{0}^{\infty}
be a smooth radial cutoff to the region
1\leq|x|\leq 2
and set
\chi_{R}(x)=\chi(\frac{x}{R})
. Using (
6.58
) at level
s_{0}
, we have

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\varlimsup_{J\to\infty}\sup_{R>0}\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\chi_{R}\cdot\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}};z_{n})(\xi_{n}-\hat{\xi}_{n}^{s_{0},J})(\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}})\right\rVert_{L^{8}L^{4}([-\tau,\tau])}=0.

(6.71)

By the local uniform convergence

(z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s_{0}}),\xi_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s_{0}}))\to(z_{\infty}^{s_{0}},\xi_{\infty}^{s_{0}})\quad\text{in}\quad\mathbb{C}\times\text{w-}H^{1},

we can also show

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|

\displaystyle\chi_{R}\cdot\mathcal{L}(\cdot,-\tau;z_{n}(\cdot+s_{n}^{s_{0}}))({\xi}_{n}-\lambda_{n}^{(s_{0},0)})(\hat{\tau}_{n}^{s_{0}})\bigr\|_{L^{8}L^{4}([-\tau,\tau])}

(6.72)
